# CLAUDE_CODE_LOG — Claude Code's internal work journal

> This file is **not** for Ishay to maintain — it is for my (Claude Code) own creation and self-update between sessions, so context isn't lost. Ishay may read it, but keeping it current is my responsibility. Update it at the end of every meaningful session.
> Language: **English** (this is a Claude-facing file, like `micro_guides/` and the templates; Hebrew appears only as data — role/module names, UI strings, §7 refs, migration names). Other truth-sources not duplicated here: `docs/PROJECT_MASTER.md` (schema/permissions/screens + §7 open questions), `../CLAUDE.md` + the directory-scoped `CLAUDE.md` files (iron rules; the DB protocol lives in `supabase/migrations/CLAUDE.md`), `../STATUS.md` (module status board, Hebrew), `docs/guides/00_roadmap.md` (operational roadmap), `docs/archive/` (pre-28/07 full versions). *(`docs/CHANGELOG.md` was frozen 23/07/2026 — archive only, never written to.)*

## Maintenance policy (read before editing)
- **"Current State"** = a snapshot **rewritten** every time to reflect reality. Not append; never let it go stale. **No internal dates (rule F4, 09/07/2026):** a date inside Current State signals dated narrative leaked in (its place is the Session Log below). The snapshot answers "what is true now" briefly (**target ~15 lines**), not "what happened when"; dense details → reference sections / journal.
- **"Session Log"** = append-only, newest first. Detail budget: the 2–3 latest sessions in full · the next ones shortened to 1–3 lines · **a session older than 3 days that isn't among the latest 2–3 → merged into a weekly/thematic super-bucket** (header `### 📦 Week DD/MM–DD/MM — topic`), after migrating any evergreen fact to the reference sections · older/generic than that — one archive line, or deleted if all its evergreen facts already moved to the reference sections.
  > 📌 **OUTSTANDING DEBT — updated 01/08/2026 (dedicated compaction session, per Ishay's 31/07 ruling below).** The 29/07–30/07 mass this note originally flagged (measured 31/07 12:50 at 34 entries / ~325 lines, out of a 534-line narrative) is now compressed into `### 📦 Week 25–30/07/2026` (also folded in the two small adjacent 28/07 + 25/07 entries — both were already "older than 3 days" and mostly redundant with the Reference paragraphs). Every evergreen fact it carried was verified to already have a durable home (`module-3.md` §9 · `module4_smart_match_research.md` §11 · `PROJECT_MASTER` §6/§7 · the Reference paragraphs below) **before** deleting, not assumed — see the compaction session's report for the spot-checks. **Remaining debt, deliberately NOT touched this pass:** the 31/07 mass. It sits inside the 3-day freshness window (rule: keep the 2–3 latest sessions in full) and a parallel builder session was still landing entries into it the same night — compressing a moving target risks a rule-16 collision. Revisit once those entries age past 3 days.
  > **Ishay's ruling 31/07/2026 (why this needed its own session):** judgement work, not cleanup — "harvest before you delete" means a separate call per entry on which evergreen facts move to the reference sections first — and **Ishay cannot review the result** (English, written for Claude), so Claude is the only gate. Mitigation that makes it safe: **every compressed entry stays fully recoverable from `git log`**, so compaction here is reversible. *(This refines, and does not cancel, `module-close` step 💾2b ownership below: module close still guarantees it happens if a dedicated session never does.)*
- **Size self-check (measure before editing):** narrative = `awk '/^## Session Log/{f=1;next} /^## Reference/{f=0} f' docs/CLAUDE_CODE_LOG.md | wc -l` (target ≤150) · snapshot = the lines between `## Current State` and the next `---` (target ~15). `regin-docs-sync` measures and flags both on every run (measure-and-flag only). **The compaction itself is OWNED by the `module-close` skill, step 💾2b** (Ishay's ruling 31/07/2026 — the flag previously had no owner and the narrative reached 457 lines). Do NOT grant the routine compaction authority: "harvest before you delete" needs to know what the NEXT modules will need, which a memoryless routine run cannot, and its English output is unreviewable by Ishay — so a routine doing it would have no gate. Any session may of course compact when asked; module close is what guarantees it happens.
- **Realistic threshold (fixed F3, 09/07/2026): the journal NARRATIVE** (Session Log only — excluding the reference sections and Current State) **over ~180 lines → compress the old tail back toward ~150.** Never compress the 2–3 newest sessions or Current State. This is a *utility-and-cost* limit: the read tool reads 2000 lines/call, and when stuck, old narrative buries the knowledge. **The metric: bound the narrative, not the reference.** *(The old "whole-file ~250→~200" threshold was never honored — it counted the exempt reference sections and thus silently "overflowed" forever; the new threshold measures what actually gets compressed.)*
- **The trigger is measured on the whole file, but compression touches only the narrative.** If the file is large because the reference grew legitimately and the narrative is already minimal — that's fine, don't sacrifice reference to get under 250.
- **The reference sections (Gotchas / Tech-debt / DB / Templates-hooks) are exempt from the count and are never compressed** — they are the long-term memory for solving problems. Keeping them current is mandatory.
- **Golden rule — "harvest before you delete":** before shrinking a session record, every fact still relevant going forward (a debt, a DB/schema decision, an open flag, a solved gotcha/trap + how) **first migrates to the appropriate reference section**, and only then do you compress. That way shortening never loses useful knowledge. Compression is the ONLY way an old dated record changes (a deliberate exception to "never rewrite records").
- **Repeated routine records = first to shrink:** green runs of `regin-health-pulse`/`regin-docs-sync` ("all green"/"no drift") are merged into one rolling line (e.g. `health-pulse: green ×5, last 07/07/2026`), not a line per run. A run that found something stays standalone.

### 08/08/2026 12:20 — **§7.44 ownership corrected on Ishay's ruling: `מ4/5/6` ⇒ `מ6`**

**The two-rulings case, resolved by him after both were brought quoted and dated.** `§7.44` (07/07/2026) assigned the atomic `project_status` trigger to modules 4/5/6; his 08/08 ruling (`spec.md` ⑱ד, post-council) says **M4 never touches that column and the trigger is built in M6.** The 08/08 ripple had reached `§6` (`🚧 מ6 ← מ4`) and **never reached §7.44** — iron rule 13(א) not run — and `⑥1` sends the blueprint to read §7.44, so M4 would have built the trigger and silently overturned the newer ruling.

🔑 **The argument that settled it is not recency, it is buildability:** §7.44 defines the trigger as firing on `assignments`**/`logistics`** changes — **logistics is module 5** (`00_roadmap.md`), and "zero logistics in M4" is Ishay's own ruling. **Half the trigger's input does not exist yet**, so building it in M4 yields something partial that looks correct and behaves wrong without erroring.

**Rule 13 ripple run in full:** §7.44 owner tag + the reason recorded · `module_05_logistics.md:17` (its `↳§7.44` note said "נבנה במודולים 4/5/6") · §6 verified already correct, no new line needed. **Two other §7 items cite §7.44 — checked, both cite its *mechanism* (atomic trigger) and not its ownership ⇒ correctly left untouched.**

### 08/08/2026 11:45 — **Three skill-scans, and the fix they forced: ⑥2 carries addresses, `SKILL.md` carries rules**

🆕 **Then Ishay's own observation reshaped the rest: *"the build skill serves all modules, the build block is per-module."*** Checked — **and it had already produced a live contradiction: ⑥2 still said "build them as they are" while the skill said "appearance only". The same rule, two copies, diverged in nine hours.** ⇒ **⑥2 was pruned to what is genuinely module-specific and not derivable — paths, the approved-mockup folder, the hand-computed oracle — and every rule now lives in `SKILL.md` alone**, with a new section stating that ⑥2 is never authority on behaviour, that no rule may be assumed present in it, that the skill wins on conflict *out loud*, and that **phase comes from the micro-guide's Active step, never from the paste-block** (⑥2 is pasted identically across DB/RLS, logic, UI, control and QA).

**And a review block handed to me for fresh eyes had three claims that did not survive checking:** *"`המוקאפ גובר על` exists once in the whole repo"* ⇒ **three** — and one of them is **`docs/guides/discovery_lessons.md`**, i.e. the precedence split is a **cross-module Discovery lesson, not a module-4 detail.** That inverted its recommendation: the skill now points at the generic home with the module's own file as local override, so a future module inherits the rule instead of lacking it. ### 08/08/2026 02:05 — **Block 6.0 executed: the approved spec is now wired into the SKILL that builds, generically for all 13 modules**

**What changed — five targeted edits, zero rewrite** (Ishay's constraint: *"אל תשכתב — הסקיל אומת כתקין"*). Four in `module-build/SKILL.md`, all four consequences of the single G1 gap measured at 02:00 (`grep -c "docs/specs"` ⇒ **0**): **①** the read order now carries `docs/specs/module_NN_*/` **before** the micro-guide, states that **tier 2 beats tier 5 per-item**, points at `spec.md §①` as the module's own reading list, and **derives the folder rather than guessing it** — with the leading-zero trap spelled out (`module_4_*` matches nothing, and an empty glob reads exactly like "no spec exists"). **②** the gap protocol now checks the spec **before** classifying technical-vs-product — without it an already-ruled weight or label gets re-decided and stamped `הכרעתי` under Claude's name. **③** *"מה ייחשב עובד"* is **harvested** from the spec's own chapter, oracle copied digit-for-digit, never re-authored — this list is written into the test *before* the code, so an invented expectation becomes the target the implementation is tuned to pass. **④** an approved mockup is **tier 2, built as drawn** — the old rule was written for tier-4 `docs/mockups/**` and, applied literally, re-draws and re-approves screens Ishay already signed. **⑤** `module-blueprint/template.md:68` — the Context Packet must carry `docs/specs/`; 5.x + mockups drop to low-tier background (the shape that produced `module-3.md:108`).

**What was deliberately preserved:** the 🔒 Plan-Mode 🗣️ gate · the STATES rule (empty · error · unknown · long text · no permission — for an approved mockup they are *read from its screen card*, not re-derived or dropped) · the 3-attempt fix cap · typed-echo · §8(h)/(i) · the three provenance tags. **Nothing was removed.**

🔑 **Genericity was Ishay's correction mid-turn, and it changed the shape of the fix:** *"רק לשים לב שזה סקיל שמתאים לכל המודולים"* → *"הוא הולך לפרומט הבנייה באותו מודול"*. ⇒ **the skill carries the MECHANISM only**, **and the module's concrete values live in its ⑥2 paste-block.** 🔴 **Correction, 08/08 11:30 — this entry originally claimed "zero occurrences of module 4, hostesses, or any oracle digit — verified by grep". The claim was false and the verification was the reason:** the grep searched `מודול 4` / `module 4` / `hostesses` / `0.67` and **never tried the underscore form `module_04`**, which was sitting in the read-order text as a worked example. **An external scan caught it; the self-check that produced the claim did not** — the standing measurement that a session cannot audit its own artifact, with one more data point. Removed in the follow-up pass; `grep -c "module_04\|module_4_"` ⇒ 0. ### 08/08/2026 (night) — **All three pre-blueprint blockers closed; the method itself written down as a reusable playbook**

**`rating` ruled** — the third and last blocker. Details in the entry below; the short version is that it was never a new product question, only a gap between two rulings Ishay had already made, and re-framing it that way closed it in one exchange.

**New file: `docs/guides/module_playbook.md`** — written on Ishay's request (*"I want to reproduce this success for the next modules — teach me what to do"*). 🔑 **The honest answer to that question is what shaped the file: it was not a prompt.** `spec.md` came out well because by the time it was written there were already three days of processes and screens underneath it that Ishay had corrected in real time — it concentrated material that had already been tested, and invented nothing. So the playbook documents **the process, not a prompt**: a mermaid flow of the three stages with his approval loops, three copy-paste blocks (one per stage), and measured durations (~2 days processes · ~2 days screens · one long session for integration). Two boxes are colour-highlighted because they are what actually separated module 4 from its predecessors — **the hand-computed number** and the **fresh-context agents**. Cross-linked from `prompt_module_discovery.md` with an explicit split of readership: *that* file is what Claude reads, *this* one is what Ishay does. ⚠️ **Caught a syntax error in the mermaid diagram before shipping** — a stray closing paren that would have broken rendering; verified by a bracket-balance check over every line rather than by eye.

**Then Ishay asked for the thing that actually makes it reproducible: his own quotes.** *"I want to reproduce the move — put in my quotes from building the processes, screens etc., all the questions, everything relevant."* **26 verbatim quotes** were pulled from the repo's own records (`discovery-log.md` · `processes-approved.md` · `screens-approved.md` · this log) — **not reconstructed from memory** — and organised **by the stage he said them in**, each paired with what it changed. 🔑 **The organising principle matters more than the count: the left column of each table is the list of questions that demonstrably worked at that stage**, so before running stage 1 or 2 he can read what he himself asked last time. Three were promoted to a closing section as the highest-yield ones — the anti-filter question (*"I wanted us to understand what you meant so I don't miss something"* — **a wrong question he can catch, a wrong filter he structurally cannot**), the reframing question (*"I'm not sure at all, maybe it's more right to…"*), and the self-challenge (*"would you do it differently? maybe I got it wrong"*), with an explicit note that **no exact wording needs remembering — the timing is what makes them work, not the phrasing.**

⚠️ **Two entries kept deliberately, because a playbook of only successes teaches the wrong thing.** (1) *"I flow with your decisions"* is filed as **delegation, not agreement** — its practical meaning is inverted from the intuition: when he delegates, the reasoning must get *stronger*, not weaker, because he has no chance to catch it. (2) *"aren't you writing in the discovery log?"* is a criticism **of Claude** — he caught an entire block going unlogged and Claude did not. It stays in.

**The playbook also records seven rules in Ishay's own words**, each with the failure it prevented — recommendation-not-menu · no work that wasn't asked for · "doesn't happen" as a closing ruling · palette from `src/` not from the previous mockup · mockup-first over verbal cards · agents are not optional · and the one worth the most: **"I'm not sure at all — maybe it's more right to stop?"** That question, asked when Claude had framed a local screen decision, is what exposed the whole sequencing question and led to the `ready`-ownership correction. It is now written down as a thing he should keep doing.

### 08/08/2026 (late evening) — **Two of the three blockers ruled; an `llm-council` run reversed the framing on one of them**

**Blocker 1 — which project statuses the overview screen shows.** Ruled: **`not_started` AND `in_progress`**, not `in_progress` alone. The bug was a closed loop: `C5 §5.5.5` births a project as `not_started` (verified in code — M3's conversion RPC writes `'not_started'` explicitly at `20260723115000…:98`), and `C5 §5.5.7` only promotes to `in_progress` **after the first staffing or logistics action** — but staffing happens on the Smart Match screen, reachable only by clicking a row on the overview. **A brand-new event needing 6 hostesses, with 0 assigned, was invisible to the person whose job is to staff it.** 🔑 **This was not overturning a ruling — the "`in_progress` only" line was written to answer *when a project LEAVES the list* (the whole surrounding paragraph is about exit); the entry direction was never examined.** No new sort needed: default order is already "missing first", and a `not_started` project is 0-of-N so it floats up on its own.

**Blocker 2 — the `ready` transition — and here the council changed the answer, not just confirmed it.** Ishay asked whether to stop and spec Modules 5+6 before continuing. Ran `llm-council`: five advisors, five blind peer reviews, chairman synthesis. **All five said don't stop; all five reviewers independently rated the Contrarian strongest** for refusing the easy analogy — reliability is a *scoring input* that degrades to zero with the screen still honest, whereas `ready` is a *state transition* the frozen spec ties to two metrics. **Then I verified the citation myself and it was sharper than the council put it:** `C5 §5.5.7` opens *"this process… serves the **projects manager**"* and is titled the **central control screen** for the project lifecycle. ⇒ **`ready` was never Module 4's to flip.** The spec line saying *"when the quota fills, the project moves to `ready` via a DB trigger"* was Module 4 reaching into another module's state machine. **Ruled: M4 writes assignment rows only, derives the staffing metric at read time (`COUNT(finally_approved) ≥ required_hostess_count` — no column, no trigger), and never touches `project_status`.** Registered as a new `🚧 מ6 ← מ4` line carrying two details that would otherwise be lost: the derivation must test **`≥` not `=`** (M4 deliberately permits over-approval, so `=` would strand an over-staffed project in `in_progress` forever), and scope-change on a `ready` project reverts it to `in_progress`. ⚠️ **Explicitly NOT a "deviation from C5" note — C5 never assigned this to M4, so the fix restores C5 rather than departing from it.**

🔑 **The methodological point worth keeping: the council's value was not its verdict.** Four of five advisors reached the right conclusion by pattern-matching ("reuse the disabled-reliability precedent") — which would have produced a *graceful-degradation* fix for what is actually an *ownership* bug. The dissenter forced the re-read that found the real answer. **And all five reviewers flagged the same omission: none of the advisors noted the decision was Ishay's to make, not the council's.**

**Ishay also asked whether the status machine itself was wrong** (*"maybe I got it wrong"*). Checked all eight values end to end against `C5` and the schema: **no change recommended.** The split is clean — four operational statuses (M4/M5/M6) then three financial (M8), with the boundary falling exactly where ownership transfers. **Two real edge cases surfaced.** (1) logistics rows derive only from non-`hostess` catalogue lines and nothing in the schema constrains a quote to contain one, so a hostess-only event would be born with zero logistics rows and "100% of logistics done" becomes 0-of-0. **✅ CLOSED by Ishay the same evening, as a reality ruling: *"there are always hostesses and badges, at minimum"*** — it is a business invariant, not a modelled case. ⇒ **No logic is built for it** (`CLAUDE.md` reality filter: never build for a case whose existence was never confirmed). Recorded in M6's debt line as a one-line implementation default only — if the derivation ever meets zero logistics rows it treats them as complete rather than dividing by zero — so nobody re-opens it as a design question later. (2) `§7` item 32, already logged: the date-passed flip to `event_finished` is unconditional, so a half-staffed event looks identical to a successful one.

**Blocker 3 (`rating` with no "unrated" value) — ✅ RULED by Ishay: the column becomes `null`.** ‏`rating int null check (rating between 1 and 5)`; `NULL` means "no impression yet", and the add-form's stars must open **empty** rather than pre-set to 3 — without that second half every new hostess is still born rated and the fix is worthless. 🔑 **Worth recording how this one was framed wrong at first and Ishay corrected it:** he pushed back — *"wasn't the whole rating thing already decided in the research?"* — and he was **right about the part he named**. The research (`§6.2`) closed *whether rating belongs in the score* (it doesn't — Nature 2025: 85% of 1–5 ratings are 5★), and ruling ① closed *what the column is called* ("impression", not "rating"). **What no one had closed was how the database expresses "not rated at all"** — and `screens-approved.md` had *already* ruled the screen shows `—` for an unrated hostess, which is **unimplementable on a `not null default 3` column.** ⇒ **This was never a new product question; it was a gap between two of his own existing rulings.** Re-framing it that way is what made it decidable in one exchange. Three measurements collapse what looked like a real trade-off: `hostesses` is **empty (0 rows)**, so there is no data to convert; **no line of code in `src/` or `scripts/` touches `rating`** (grep returned zero), so nothing breaks; and **a migration on this table is already being written** — the §7.64 surrogate-key change is Module 4's first migration — so "it saves a migration", the only argument for the alternative, is simply false here. The alternative (read `=3` as "unrated") destroys what the field exists for: it is labelled **"the manager's impression"**, an explicitly subjective human judgement, and collapsing `3` would **silently erase a real rating** and show `—` for a hostess she did rate — one of five grades stops existing. ⚠️ **And the fix is only half a fix without the form change:** the add-form mockup pre-selects 3 stars, so even with a nullable column every new hostess would still be born rated. Stars must start empty, and the field stays optional (consistent with `processes-approved.md §א1`, which marks only name · phone · ID · hourly rate as required).

### 08/08/2026 (evening) — **Module 4 Discovery stage 3 CLOSED: `spec.md` written, the research doc moved into the module folder, 3 missing DB requirements registered**

**What changed.** `docs/specs/module_04_hostesses/spec.md` created — the file `docs/CLAUDE.md` requires of every spec folder and that had never existed, leaving the Discovery→blueprint link with no defined endpoint. Deliberately **narrow** (Ishay's ruling 07/08, 5 items — not the cancelled 12-chapter PRD): a blueprint reading list · a 3-line intro · the four contract items · "what counts as working" + "what is deliberately still open" · and chapter 12 ("what the blueprint must not guess") **copied**, not rewritten. Plus one chapter that is not a summary of anything: **the algorithm explained in Hebrew**, ~40 lines — four layers, why the gate precedes the score, what the damping does, the two distinct distance numbers, the two chip families, why sort-angles and not filters. `module4_smart_match_research.md` `git mv`'d into the module folder; **17 path pointers updated across 10 files**, verified by grep returning only the two deliberately-untouched ones (dated journal entries; and `two-weeks-2026-08-06.md`, which a parallel session had edited 24 minutes earlier — skipped rather than fought over, per rule 16).

**The real work was contract item 3 — the hand-computed number**, and it is the thing this module did not have. Module 3's chain (`6,319` appearing once in the spec ⇒ 33× in its build guide ⇒ permanently in `e2e/smoke-anchors.json`) had no module-4 equivalent, because C5 contains no computed output value for these sections. Built a full Smart Match case — 5 hostesses, 2 excluded by two *different* gate conditions, company average `C=0.60`, reliability component off so weights renormalize to `0.40/0.65` and `0.25/0.65` — and computed it by hand from `§11`: **נועה 0.67 · מיכל 0.66 · דנה 0.64**. The case is built so each number *proves* something: proximity flips the raw ranking against a candidate with double the response rate (so the component demonstrably matters), the `1-of-1` hostess ranks *below* the `6-of-7` one (acceptance test #1 of §11.10), and the fairness lever reverses the pre-lever order (proving it ran, at the right stage). ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT written: which tests to implement** — the blueprint's per-step `Verify` field owns that; the spec supplies the expectation, not the test.

**The cross-check found eight things, and three were real gaps in the DB registry** *(this is the failure class the check exists for, and it is now the third recorded occurrence)*: 🔴 **`projects.lat`/`lng` were registered nowhere** — `db_roadmap.md` had three `projects` rows and none was this — while `processes-approved.md` declared them "already ruled, awaiting execution". They are dependency #1 in `§11.6`; without event-side coordinates there is no distance, which drops **both** the proximity component **and** the 80 km layer-1 gate. A blueprint plans migrations from `db_roadmap`, so it would simply have omitted them. Also registered: the **two fairness-lever numbers as `params`** (they were hardcoded, and the 8-week cap is used in *two* places — the lever and the "worked recently" chip), and the **exposure log** (dependency #7, zero mentions anywhere, marked non-blocking so a blueprint rejection is a conscious call rather than a silent omission).

**Ishay ruled on the lever itself.** `§11.2` gave it `0.05` = up to **+40%**, while the two sections that justify it call it *"a **small** capped spray bonus"* and *"a **small** cold-start guarantee is enough; a big thumb on the scale buys little"* — the number contradicted its own rationale in two places, and the hand-computed case demonstrated it reversing an entire ranking. **Ruled `0.02`** (+16%). Recorded honestly: the display order comes out identical under both values, which makes the *ordering* claim a better regression anchor than the digits. ⚠️ **And for the record: `0.05` had never been brought to Ishay** — it is not among the eight sub-decisions of 29–30/07 nor the three additions in §9.11; only its *source* was ruled (ruling ⑥, "when she last **worked**", not "when invited"). Two further gaps closed with it: a hostess who has **never** worked gets `0` weeks, not the cap (the damping already handles newcomers), and the rounding order is pinned (`round(score × lever, 2)` — lever on the raw score, one rounding at the end) because `§11.2` named both and said neither came first.

**Two documentation ripples that had stalled, caught by measurement rather than by reading.** (1) The layer-2 pin tag `מצוינת אצל הלקוח הזה` is required by `processes-approved.md §ב1` (*"floats to the top **with a visible tag**"*) and listed in `§11.5` as a display element — and `grep "מצוינת" screens-approved.md` returned **0**. Its wording, placement and behaviour did not exist anywhere; Ishay ruled to specify it now, since layer-2 code ships with module 4 regardless. (2) **Surface 5's mockup still carried `_DRAFT` in its filename and sat outside `approved/`**, while the state table, `next-session-prompt.md` and commit `548eaa8`'s own title all said approved. Rather than resolve it by commit archaeology, the mockup was rendered and shown to Ishay — he confirmed, and it moved. 🔑 **The lesson, because this module has now been burned by it twice: an approval that fails to reach all three places (state table · section header · filename) is indistinguishable from a non-approval** — and here it would have dropped the one surface that writes to the DB with no login out of the blueprint's reading list entirely. Two sibling stale markers (3ב's header, 3ד's internal pointer) were straightened at the same time.

**Three fresh-context agents, deliberately given three different angles — and the third was the one that mattered.** Angle 1: *"which claims here have no source"* + *"what does this spec not mention that the blueprint will need"*. Angle 2: recompute the anchor number from `§11` alone, blind to the claim. Angle 3: **simulate a build session restricted to reading ONLY the files on the spec's own reading list**, and record every point where it would have to guess. **Agent 2 confirmed all fourteen values and the display order independently — the anchor is safe.** But between them they found things that reading could not:

- 🔴 **The fifth gate condition (declared unavailability) was never rippled into `§11.2`.** Ruled 05/08 — *after* `§11` was written — and `§11` opens with *"read **only** this section to implement"*. A compliant build session would have shipped a four-condition gate and invited hostesses who had declared themselves abroad. Since response rate is **40% of the score**, they would then decline four invitations and be recorded as unreliable — the exact harm the unavailability feature was added to prevent. Written into `§11.2`.
- 🔴 **`C` ("the same ratio over all hostesses in the window") is genuinely ambiguous**, and the two readings diverge: company-wide gives `0.60` and a clean order; candidate-pool gives `0.65` and **a tie at the top**, broken by an md5 whose inputs the contract item never supplies. Pinned in `§11.3`, with the reason — a per-pool `C` makes one hostess's score depend on who else passed the gate, which is the same defect the fixed goalpost exists to prevent.
- 🔴 **Two claims in the newly-written spec were simply wrong, and both were mine.** The locked `pending` label was set to `ממתינה` on the strength of *"that's what the mockup shows"* — measured, the rendered tag is **`ממתינה למענה` (×5)**, and bare `ממתינה` appears once, in a summary table cell. And the pin tag was described as *"appears in the mockup"* — it does not: `.chip.pin` is CSS-defined and unused, and the mockup says outright *"the 'מצוינת' tag does not exist yet"*. 🔑 **Both were rule-cited-correctly, conclusion-drawn-from-memory. Neither was catchable by re-reading — only by grep.**
- 🔴 **The double-booking constraint is not expressible as written.** Three documents demand `partial UNIQUE (hostess_id, event_date)`; `assignments` has no date column, the date lives on `projects`, and a UNIQUE index cannot join. The ruling forbids the easy escape ("DB constraint, not a code check"). Registered with both viable mechanisms.
- 🔴 **`rating int not null default 3` has no value meaning "unrated"** while three sources require showing `—`. Registered as a product decision, not a build choice.
- Plus: **`event_id` in the tie-break formula is a phantom column** (it is `projects.project_id`) and the sort direction was unstated — both fixed; **the reading list was materially short for the two things a build session does first** (write a migration, match existing code patterns) — six entries added, each justified by a quote from a file that *was* on the list; and **the email layer had no home at all** — three seeded templates never named, three orphan placeholders with no data source, and a fourth required template registered nowhere.

🔑 **The methodological lesson, because it cost real defects both directions:** the two errors in my own text were found by the agent that was forbidden from reading anything outside the list, not by the two that could read everything. **Constraining a reviewer to the consumer's actual field of view is what surfaces what the consumer cannot see.**

**Verification.** `check:docs-structure` **green, 28 files, zero findings** — after fixing a pre-existing `§10 after §11` ordering defect in the research doc that only became visible once the move brought the file into scope. Every `table.column` in `spec.md` resolved: those that exist are in `schema.sql` (including the M3 delta block at ~502-505); those that do not are registered in `db_roadmap.md` — checked per item, not per declaration. `check:docs-structure` now flags `§10 appears after §11` inside the research doc — a pre-existing ordering defect that only became visible because the move brought the file into the checker's scope. ⚠️ **Not committed** — Ishay asked to hold off while a parallel session works.

- **Ishay immediately sharpened it further, and was right: shouldn't the tests-to-implement live in the spec — or does the blueprint already know that on its own?** Confirmed the second: the blueprint's own step format is `Goal / Files / What / **Verify**`, so verification per build step is already its job; listing tests in the spec would both duplicate that and rot. **But this exposed that "contract item #3" had been described imprecisely all along — it is not a test.** It is the *expected value* a test checks against. The distinction is load-bearing: a blueprint session can invent a test, but it cannot invent the correct Smart Match score — that requires reading the formulas and computing by hand. If it computes the expectation itself, the resulting test compares the code to its own logic, which is the repo's own "a guard never seen failing is not a guard" failure. **Restated the boundary: the spec supplies one hand-computed expectation; the blueprint builds the test from it.** Narrows `spec.md` further than the version approved minutes earlier — recorded in `next-session-prompt.md` so the next session inherits the sharper scope, not the looser one.

Title`, but it also matches `## 3.1`, extracting `3` from both `## 3.1` and `## 3.2` and reporting them as a duplicate `§3`. So the world-practice discipline lives **only** in `prompt_module_discovery.md` §5 — a file loaded solely when pasted into a Discovery session — while his four tests apply to every session including build. Real gap, not a wording nit. *(The academic/no-real-client half **is** already in root `CLAUDE.md`'s 🎓 block, so it was not duplicated.)*
- **He then raised the sharpest instruction-design question of the session: "דוגמאות עוזרות או מצמצמות את מי שקורא?"** Answered from measurements already in this repo rather than from principle: the palette-grep once named five color families explicitly and was corrected 07/08 for exactly this reason (*"רשימה סגורה מאשרת את מה שכבר ידוע ואינה מסוגלת לגלות משפחה שישית"*), and `_shared/discipline.md` already carries the general form ("never hand a checker an expected number — only a measurement method"); conversely the `login_rpc_calls` precedent, an example of a *case* rather than an *answer*, is what generated a genuine new finding on 07/08. **Generalization: an example naming the ANSWER closes the reader's search; an example naming a CASE opens it.** Applied it to my own draft and found two of my own biases: a closed three-item list of "problems we don't have" (load / multi-tenancy / regulatory audit) — the palette failure repeating — rewritten as a test with the three marked as illustrations; and "the four failure modes," which reads as a complete taxonomy when all four were measured in a single day — changed to "four measured so far, not a closed taxonomy." Both found only because he asked, which is itself the honest data point.
- **Ishay: "חשוב שלא יהיו סתירות בכללים" — so I audited my own addition against the rest of iron rule 1 before and after writing, and found two contradictions I had introduced.** (1) My "יש עוגן ⇒ הכרע לבד" sat five lines under the pre-existing "שאלות פתוחות מוכרעות **רק איתו**… אותו דבר לכל החלטת מוצר או עיצוב" — readable as a licence to self-rule product decisions, the exact yes-man failure rule 1 exists to prevent. Fixed with an explicit boundary clause: the anchor rule changes the **form** of what reaches him, never **who owns the ruling**. (2) Found on the second pass, after writing: "אל תשאל שאלה פתוחה" contradicts both the rule's own title ("**שאל** כשלא חד-משמעי") and my own case ③ ("never decided ⇒ ask, with a recommendation"). What was meant is that an **empty** question is forbidden, not a question — surfaced to Ishay with the corrected wording rather than silently patched. Also swept and reported clean: test ④ vs the truth hierarchy, test ① vs rules 5/12 (DB), test ③ vs rule 8 (design), and "his ruling = an anchor" vs case ② (he ruled twice).
- **Net effect on root `CLAUDE.md`:** iron rule 1 grew by ~28 lines. Flagged the bloat honestly rather than letting it pass (the file was deliberately pruned 28/07 from 17 rules to 5), and offset part of it by deleting a verbatim quote that duplicated the line directly above it. **Both self-introduced contradictions are now fixed in the file** — the ownership boundary and the empty-question wording, the latter approved and written last.
- **What the rule block finally contains** (all inside iron rule 1, numbering still frozen): the anchor requirement and what to do when there is none · the ownership boundary (form of what reaches him, never who rules) · "an anchor he cannot check himself is a claim in disguise" · his four tests verbatim plus four operational questions under them · the three-case contradiction protocol including the ruled-twice-and-forgot case · four named failure modes marked as measured-so-far rather than a closed taxonomy.
- **Ishay tested the written rule by restating it back in his own words — and the comparison found a real defect: *"או שלא כתבת טוב או שלא הבנת מה רציתי."*** Diagnosis was the former, and specific: I had filed "world practice / business-sensible" as **check ① of four** — a filter applied to something I am already considering building — whereas in his framing it is the thing I go **fetch when there is no internal anchor**, precisely so he never receives an empty question. My wording ("אין עוגן ⇒ אמור שאין") left him holding exactly the pain the rule exists to remove. Rewritten: **no internal anchor ⇒ go get an external one** (world practice with a cited source + business sense at our real scale: 50 hostesses, 2–5 per shift, no real client) **and come back with a recommendation** — explicitly not a substitute for his ruling, which still arrives *with* the recommendation rather than instead of it.
- **A second gap surfaced from his restatement, which I had never written at all:** purely technical-execution choices with no product meaning (variable name, query shape, code phrasing, a log fix) were passing through the "is there an anchor?" gate, which manufactures over-asking on things that were always mine. Added as an explicit no-anchor-needed default, bounded by "an anchor is required for anything someone sees or feels."
- **He then added the requirement that makes a world-proposal actionable for him: it must state what it practically costs to implement here, and how complex** — *"אם לא מורכב סיכוי גבוה שפשוט אסכים למה שימליץ כי לי אין ידע מספיק רחב בנושא."* Proposed with a trap named that he did not ask for: since he will likely approve whatever reads as cheap, **the complexity estimate is in practice the thing that decides — not him** ⇒ an understated estimate is a silent ruling, so it must also state what it does **not** include (tests, ripples to other screens, what could break), and "simple" may only be said after checking, not after guessing. This is the same "invisible filtering" failure already named as the top mine in the Discovery prompt.
- **Ishay pushed twice more, and both pushes were right.** (1) *"ב-50 דיילות — להגיד את זה זה מספיק?"* — no: scale alone filters only volume-driven conventions, while the constraints that actually kill world practice here are the absent hostess app, the absence of any real usage data to calibrate from, a single developer, and the 19/09 deadline. (2) *"להתאים למערכת שלנו זה עמום וחשבתי שאתה תפרק את זה"* — correct, and it is the same failure class as the "5-line status header" instruction that `grep` proved had never once produced output. Decomposed into four fit questions, **each with a verification target rather than a feeling**: channel (no hostess app — everything routes through the manager) · data (`schema.sql`/`db_roadmap.md`, read not recalled) · role (`PROJECT_MASTER §3` — five roles, one person per domain) · volume (50 / 2–5 ⇒ re-sort rather than filter). Chose those four because **each has already killed a world convention in this project** — data killed the reliability component, volume produced the sort-angles-not-filters ruling, channel is why surface 5 exists at all.
- **Then: "אבל זה הקובץ המתאים?" — and the honest measurement said partly no.** Iron rule 1 had grown to ~40 lines inside a file deliberately pruned on 28/07 from 17 rules to 5, i.e. one rule occupying ~20% of the only always-loaded file. Split by **when each part fires**, not by topic: the core decision protocol (anchor test · no-anchor behavior · ownership boundary · contradiction protocol · the four named failures · his four tests verbatim) fires every single turn and **stays in root**; the world-practice machinery (four fit questions · two-part answer · implementation price tag) fires only at one rare moment and **moved to `_shared/discipline.md`**, which already owns exactly this genre — its existing table is "which claim gets verified where." Root keeps one pointer line. Net: root sheds ~8 lines. **Named the tradeoff rather than selling the move:** `discipline.md` loads via 9/10 repo skills but **not** in a free session, so this trades certainty for leanness — acceptable here only because the world-fit check happens at a moment when documents are being read anyway, unlike the core rule which must be in front of the eyes always.
- **The rule then got its first unplanned field test, and failed it — usefully.** Ishay pasted a parallel session's summary of "how we work together" and asked whether it matched what we meant. It reproduced the anchor half faithfully but dropped the price tag, his four verbatim tests, and the four failure names — and, more seriously, titled its own section *"מה אין לי מושג עליו ואתה חייב להכריע"*. **Diagnosed the cause as my wording, not that session's reading:** the ownership-boundary clause said product/design rulings "arrive digested, with evidence" and **never once used the word "recommendation"** — which appears only in case ③ of the contradiction protocol. A reasonable reader concludes "his call, I supply facts," i.e. exactly the empty-handed hand-off the rule was written to ban. Ishay's own reaction was to go to the mechanism, not the instance: *"אז הניסוח שמה מבלבל כנראה וצריך לסדר את זה."* Two clarifications written: **(a)** "his ruling" ≠ "I have no opinion" — the item arrives digested, with evidence **and a reasoned recommendation**, with anchor and recommendation explicitly labelled; **(b)** "never decide on a hunch" ≠ "never state an opinion" — with no anchor you still say what you think and why, tagged as an assumption (`הנחתי`); the ban is on *ruling* by it, not on *saying* it. Without (b) the rule pushes toward the barren "no anchor, you decide" — the politest possible way to hand the work back to him.
- **Two mechanism facts named plainly rather than glossed** (Ishay: *"כל הפואנטה שנייצר פה התנהגות משמעת אוטומטית"*): a running session does **not** see `CLAUDE.md` edits made after it started — which, together with the skills-only load path of `discipline.md`, fully explains the gaps in that session's summary; and **loading is automatic, compliance is not** — this is text, not a hook, no mechanism can judge whether a finding rests on an anchor, so the only detector remains Ishay noticing he had to write a paragraph instead of "מה העוגן?". Recommended **against** patching the running session by hand (a manual paste is a patch, not a mechanism) since its remaining work is nine mechanical card updates where the missing clauses barely apply.
- **I first recommended deferring the compaction of iron rule 1 and Ishay pushed back — "למה לא לעשות עכשיו לא הבנתי? סוכן חיצוני לא נחשב?" — and he was right.** My whole objection had been self-review (measured 0-of-5 self-catch), which a fresh-context agent removes entirely; I had conflated "I shouldn't compact this" with "nobody should compact this today." Retracted, with one caveat kept: the agent **reports, does not write**, since `CLAUDE.md` is the most load-bearing file in the repo. Also caught myself before contaminating it: the 7.9 plan item I had just written **contains my own conclusions** about which passages duplicate — handing that to a checker is precisely the "never hand a checker the expected answer" ban in `_shared/discipline.md`, so the agent got the file and the criteria only.
- **The agent found four things I had missed, three of them defects I introduced today.** (1) **Two different definitions of "עוגן" in the same rule** — the opening list names five kinds, the "anchor he cannot check" clause names four *different* ones, and nothing says the first is "enough for you to decide" while the second is "enough to show him"; a fresh session must reconcile them. (2) **`הצעה-שלא-ביקש` was named as one of four failure modes with no rule anywhere behind it** — three of the four names point at a clause, that one pointed at nothing. (3) **`*(③ למטה)*` resolves to the wrong item** — two separate ①②③④ lists sit below it and the reader meets the wrong one first. (4) The duplication I had found was **triple, not double** (a third instance at the external-anchor clause). It also **corrected one of my findings** — the §7/product/design re-listing is not worth merging, since the second occurrence is a scoping qualifier carrying its own back-pointer.
- **It independently reached the same conclusion I had on the one decision that mattered, by the same reasoning:** do **not** move the four execution questions to `_shared/discipline.md`, because that file is never auto-loaded and a free-form session would keep demanding an anchor while silently dropping the feasibility, precedent and pattern checks. And it supplied a justification for the rule's length that I had not thought of: **rule 1 is the only iron rule in the file with no mechanical enforcer** — 15 has a Stop hook, 16 a session-start hook, 10 has git, 6 has gitleaks — so for a rule enforced by text alone, text is the entire budget.
- **Fixed on Ishay's approval (defects, not compaction):** the two anchor lists reconciled with an explicit "yours to decide vs. his to check" clause · the fourth failure name given the rule it was missing, anchored to his own words from today (*"אין סיבה להוסיף עבודה גם אם זה שיפור נחמד"*) · the broken cross-reference now names its target in words · and a rotted line-number citation (`db_roadmap.md:119`, the row has since moved) replaced with a grep anchor per the rule's own instruction to remove numbers rather than update them. **Compaction of the triplication deliberately NOT done** — ~3 lines saved out of 55, low return and highest risk at the end of a long session; it stays owned by plan item 7.9.
- **The last open item became the rule's first self-application, at Ishay's prompting: *"קרא את כלל 1 ובא נראה אם תצליח להחליט."*** The question was whether the four failure names should stay worded as vocabulary **for Ishay** or be reframed as a pre-send **self-check for Claude**. Ran the rule's own gates rather than picking on taste: **test ② (מתחבר, לא מבודד — "who already decided something similar? find the precedent")** settles it, and the precedent is unambiguous — every other rule in `CLAUDE.md`, and the whole of `_shared/discipline.md`, is phrased as an imperative to Claude; the four names were **the only item in the file written as a service to the reader instead of an instruction**, which by test ②'s own words is *"סתירה לפתור, לא בחירה חופשית"*. Test ③ agreed (keeping it would preserve a lone exception in a file of imperatives) and test ④ gave a one-sentence justification needing no citation ("the file is written for Claude, so its lines tell Claude what to do"). Not a product decision, fully reversible ⇒ **decided alone, anchor shown, override left open** — exactly what the rule prescribes for this case. Reworded to `ולפני שאתה שולח — ודא שאינך עושה אחד מארבעת אלה`, with a closing line noting the names double as Ishay's catching vocabulary. **The trade-off turned out to be false: the names are identical either way; only the framing sentence changed, so the instruction now fires without losing the glossary.**
- **Left open at session end, not by me:** a parallel session had produced nine copy-gap findings against screens 1/2/4, every one classified as "already decided elsewhere" with a dated anchor and **zero** items needing a new ruling from Ishay. Reviewed but explicitly **not endorsed** — I verified none of the nine, and under the rule just written an unverified anchor is not an anchor. Two cautions given instead: (a) a real rule-16 collision risk, since I renumbered `screens-approved.md`'s sections today and that session's plan may rest on a pre-edit read ⇒ it must re-read the file before touching it; (b) nine-for-nine with zero questions is also the exact shape overfitting produces, since the new rule creates pressure to *find* an anchor ⇒ recommended Ishay spot-check two anchors of his own choosing (suggested the two resting on `discovery-log.md`, the least-audited of the three sources) before greenlighting.

- **Ishay then asked for the concrete tradeoffs of building that mechanical check, and a recommendation.** Gave five specific, non-generic risks (not a boilerplate risk list): many instruction files are deliberately unreachable-by-design (pasted by hand, e.g. this very prompt file); `docs/plans/` is *supposed* to go stale, so a naive checker can't distinguish "died correctly" from "died with unfinished work inside"; real build/maintenance cost on an already-strained hook/CI setup; **the check only catches zero-pointer cases, not read-but-abandoned-under-fatigue cases** — which is what actually happened to `discovery-log.md` per the agent's own finding, since `module-blueprint/template.md` *does* point to the specs folder and things still slipped; skill-loading is trigger-phrase-based (semantic), not a hard graph edge, so a textual reachability check can't confirm a trigger will actually fire. **Recommendation given, not just options:** don't build a general reachability graph — too expensive, too many false positives for a deadline-pressured solo project. Do build a narrow extension of the *already-existing* `check-docs-updated.sh`/`check:docs-structure` mechanism, checking one specific known list (does `prompt_module_discovery.md` mention every still-open `docs/plans/*.md`) — cheap, reuses existing infra, would have caught exactly today's incident. ### 07/08/2026 (early morning) — **Dependabot's 8 auto-opened PRs all targeted `main` by default (no `target-branch` set), and I merged 7 of them there before noticing — a process violation I own, now being corrected on a dedicated branch.**
- **What happened.** After M2 landed on `main`, Ishay approved reviewing and merging Dependabot's 8 open PRs (patch/minor version bumps: 3 GitHub-Actions-tool bumps, 5 npm package bumps). All 8 had `base: main` — the repo's default branch, since `.github/dependabot.yml` had no `target-branch` override. **I merged 7 of them (`#14,15,16,17,18,20,21`) via `gh pr merge` without checking each PR's base branch first**, which pushed 7 dependency-bump commits straight onto `main` — bypassing both iron rule 10 ("never push directly to `main`/`dev`") and the milestone-only merge policy for `main` (`docs/guides/00_roadmap.md §4`), on the same night that policy was itself reinforced by the M2 merge.
- **How it surfaced.** PR `#19` (`tailwindcss` core, 4.3.2→4.3.3 — the one package here that actually affects rendered output, not just tooling) hit a real lockfile conflict against `main` after PR `#18` merged. I resolved it locally (clean 3-way merge, `npm ci` clean, full `gate` exit 0, plus a live visual check via the browser preview: `dir="rtl"` on `<html>`, `--primary: #0d9488` intact, login button rendered correctly) — but before pushing, checked *why* GitHub still reported it conflicting, and discovered via `gh api .../pulls/N -q '.base.ref'` that every PR's base was `main`, not `dev`. `git log origin/dev..origin/main` confirmed: 7 bump commits sitting on `main` that `dev` never received — `dev`, which should always lead, was now trailing `main` on dependency versions.
- **Damage assessment, stated plainly to Ishay before touching anything further:** not a security or data risk (every merge had full CI green, including `gitleaks`), but a real process inversion — `main` had advanced outside the milestone flow. Reported the exact finding and a two-part fix, and **stopped for explicit approval** given it touched `main`'s history and I'd already made one mistake this session; Ishay approved with "מאשר".
- **The fix, on `ishay/dev-parity-dependabot` (branched fresh from `dev`, not stacked on the already-merged branch, per the parallel-session/dead-branch rule):** merged `origin/main` into it (brings the 7 stray bump commits back into `dev`'s lineage, clean 3-way merge, no conflicts since docs-only `dev` commits and package-file-only `main` commits never touched the same lines) — **then** applied the `tailwindcss` core bump on top and ran `npm install --package-lock-only` **once**, so the lockfile reflects everything in a single consistent pass rather than three separate resolutions. Added `.github/dependabot.yml`'s missing `target-branch: dev` to both ecosystems so this can't recur silently. Verified: `npm ci` clean, `npm run gate` exit 0 (all 9 steps, 413 tests, `check:docs-structure` 26 files / zero findings). Opened PR `#23` with `base: dev` **and explicitly verified `.baseRefName == "dev"` via the API before trusting it** — the exact check I skipped the first time.
- 🔑 **The lesson, stated once so it doesn't need re-deriving:** a PR's base branch is not implied by its content or by "where it obviously should go" — Dependabot's silent default (repo default branch) is exactly the kind of gap that produces a correct-looking, green-CI merge to the wrong place. **Check `.base.ref` before merging any bot-opened or unfamiliar-provenance PR, every time, not just when something looks off.**

### 07/08/2026 01:53 — **Milestone M2 reached: `dev` merged to `main` (PR #13), tagged `milestone-2` — after an overnight platform outage was investigated to completion rather than assumed away.**
- **What happened, in sequence.** Ishay asked to merge PR #12 (the doc-fix work) to `dev`. My local `npm run gate` was green (exit 0, all 9 steps), so I merged and pushed. Ishay then asked *"ממה השומר מגן?"* ("what does the guard protect against?") — a question that exposed a claim I had made minutes earlier was wrong: I had told him my local gate "covers more than CI", when in fact it only supersets **one** of CI's three jobs (`Lint·Test·Build`); the other two (`Edge function type-check` via Deno, and `Secret scan` via gitleaks) are **not** run locally at all — gitleaks isn't even installed. I corrected this to him explicitly rather than letting the earlier overstatement stand uncorrected.
- **The actual finding: GitHub Actions was in a platform-wide outage, not a repo problem.** `curl githubstatus.com/api/v2/summary.json` showed `Actions => major_outage`, an active incident since `2026-08-06T15:22:49Z`, description matching our symptom verbatim: *"Many push and pull request events are not yet triggering new workflow runs"* — with GitHub's own incident log stating only ~15% of webhooks were being processed at one point. This directly explained why pushing PR #12's merge to `dev` triggered nothing. **Ruled out first, so the outage explanation wasn't reached for prematurely:** Actions enabled at repo level (`allowed_actions: all`), workflow `state: active`, no `paths:` filter that could exclude the changed files, zero queued/stuck runs, zero runs on the branch. Branch protection API returned 403 (private repo, free plan — a product limitation, not evidence either way).
- **Recommendation at the time: wait for the outage to clear before merging to `main`,** specifically because `main` is the one merge point where `gitleaks` actually runs, and there was no reason to skip that check when the delay cost was hours, not days — `main` only merges at milestones per `docs/guides/00_roadmap.md`.
- **This session, resumed same-day: verified the outage was over, then proved it rather than trusting the status page alone.** `githubstatus.com` now reports 0 unresolved incidents / all systems operational. But status-page text is not proof a specific repo's webhooks are flowing again — so before touching `main`, opened PR #13 (`dev`→`main`) and watched `gh pr checks 13 --watch` live: all 5 checks passed, including **gitleaks running for the first time on this content** (it never ran on PR #12 during the outage). That live pass — not the status page — is what the merge decision rests on.
- **One anomaly noted and not chased further, because it didn't block the decision:** the `push` event for PR #12's merge commit to `dev` never triggered its own CI run (checked via `check-runs` API — only Vercel's check exists on that commit), even ~30 min after the outage's official all-clear and after a `pull_request`-triggered run on the same SHA had already succeeded. Likely residual webhook backlog drainage, but not confirmed. **Not a blocker for the `dev`→`main` merge**, since PR #13's own checks — a fresh, live, current-moment trigger — passed directly.
- **Merged and tagged, mirroring the milestone-1 precedent exactly** (`git show milestone-1` was read first to match form): PR #13 merged → `main` at `a6edfb0` → verified `46836b0` (this session's earlier commit) is an ancestor via `git merge-base --is-ancestor` → tagged `milestone-2` with an annotated message naming the PR and the acceptance criterion (6,319 ₪) → pushed the tag → `docs/guides/00_roadmap.md`'s M2 row marked ✅ with the tag/PR citation, matching M1's row format exactly rather than inventing new wording.
- 🔑 **The generalizable lesson, and why this is worth a full entry rather than one line:** a red/absent CI status has (at least) two very different causes — "the code is broken" and "the checker itself isn't running" — and treating the second as the first would have meant debugging a repo that was never broken. The discriminating test was not reading more status text but **causing a fresh event and watching it complete live**, which is also the same discipline `src/CLAUDE.md`'s "a guard you never watched fail is not a guard" rule already asks for, applied here to the guard's own liveness rather than to what it checks.

### 07/08/2026 — **The two `claude-md-improver` findings that were filtered out by mistake on 06/08 are now closed — and the round's real product is that BOTH the prompt I was handed AND my own first draft carried a false claim about the repo. Docs only; no code, no migrations, no DB.**
- **What landed.** ① `docs/CLAUDE.md:37` *"שבעת סקילי-הריפו"* → the number **removed**, replaced by a pointer to `docs/toolbox.md` (per `_shared/discipline.md`: *"REMOVE the number, do not update it"*). Measured: **10** skill dirs carry `SKILL.md`, **8** are tracked, 2 are Ishay's external trial installs — and `toolbox.md` covers all ten (`:52-53` + `:76-77`), so the pointer is complete, not partial. ② `docs/CLAUDE.md:38`'s hook enumeration → also replaced by a pointer, to `.claude/settings.json`. ③ Two mines into the root `CLAUDE.md` "עובדות סביבה" (Ishay's scope ruling — he pruned that file deliberately on 28/07, so this was his call, not mine).
- 🔴 **My own draft was wrong, and it is the finding worth keeping.** I first wrote — and showed Ishay — *"`check:context` and `check:docs-structure` run nowhere automatically."* **False for `check:context`:** `.claude/hooks/session-start-context.sh:50` runs `node scripts/check-context.mjs --quiet` on **every** session start, and the hook even documents why (*"fail-open … זה הקשר, לא שער"*). I would have written a false claim about the repo **into the always-loaded root file** — the exact defect class this whole task exists to fix. It surfaced only because Ishay said *"בצע עוד בדיקות"* a third time; two prior passes over the same wording did not catch it. **Corroborates `discipline.md`'s own line: a file is not audited by re-reading it.** Corrected measurement: `check-docs-structure.mjs` has **exactly one reference in the entire repo** — its own `package.json` line. Zero automatic callers.
- 🔴 **And the prompt that assigned this task asserted `npm run gate` is "the gate that blocks merge". It is not.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (job named `quality-gate`) runs `lint · format:check · dup · deadcode · audit · test:run · build` **as separate steps and never invokes `gate`**. So `check:context` (informational only) and `check:docs-structure` (nothing) are outside CI, E2E and smoke are not in CI at all, and `test:e2e` is `playwright test --grep-invert בדיקת-עשן` — it **silently excludes** `e2e/smoke.spec.js`. Both facts are now mines in the root file. *(The prompt disclosed its own numbers were unverified and told me to re-measure. That disclosure is what made the catch cheap — worth repeating in future prompts.)*
- **7.7② closed differently than the plan specified, deliberately and with Ishay's ruling.** The plan said "fix three→four". Measured: the list was wrong **twice** — it omitted `block-shell-dialect-mixup` **and** counted `session-start-context`, which does not block at all (`exit 0`). So the enumeration was removed rather than corrected. The plan also asked for a line on what that hook blocks and how to escape it — **not written**: the hook prints a full translation table and the escape itself when it fires, so re-documenting it duplicates a message that already arrives at the right moment. **What replaced it is the part the hook does NOT say:** searching *for* those cmdlet names is also blocked, because `:71` reads the `|` before the name as a verb position. Measured live — the hook fired on my own `grep` this session.
- **Bookkeeping, to stop repeated work:** 7.7③ was found **already done** (`src/CLAUDE.md:11` now carries `grep "@/supabaseClient" src/` instead of the count; the `CustomersPage:289` citation and the colour tally are gone). 7.7① is still open and its cited line has already rotted from `:5` to `:4`. Both recorded in the plan file. ⏸️ **Found and NOT fixed, by Ishay's explicit scope choice — recorded here because `docs/plans/` dies at merge and this must outlive it:** `module-close/template.md:50` and `:62` both enumerate `gate` as `verify+dup+deadcode+audit+check:context`, **omitting `check:docs-structure`** (`:62` also duplicates a sentence); and `block-shell-dialect-mixup.sh:99`'s pipe-guard list names `check:context` but not `check:docs-structure`, so `npm run check:docs-structure | tail` is the one gate command that slips the exit-code mask. **Same disease as `:37` — a hard-coded enumeration that went stale.** Editing a hook is a behaviour change, not a doc fix, so it needs its own ruling.
- ✅ **SECOND PASS, same session — Ishay said "כן" to closing the three items I had left open. All three landed, and 7.7 is now fully closed.** ① `src/modules/03_quotes/CLAUDE.md` header → `🔒 מודול סגור ומוזג (PR #10 · PR #11)`, matching the two sibling module files verbatim; the "this file is deliberately partial, it grows with each step (rule 15)" clause was dropped because a closed module does not grow. *(Its cited line had already rotted `:5`→`:4`, and both merges were re-verified fresh: `792456f` and `9b319df` are both ancestors of `dev`.)* ② `module-close/template.md:50` and `:62` — the hard-coded `gate` enumeration replaced by a pointer to `package.json` §scripts in both places, the duplicated sentence at `:62` removed, **and `:50` corrected on a second count**: it called `gate` *"the project's blocking gate"*, which conflates it with CI. It is the **module-close** gate and a **superset** of CI. ③ `block-shell-dialect-mixup.sh:99`.
- 🛡️ **The hook change was PROVEN TO FAIL, not just to pass — per `src/CLAUDE.md`'s "a guard you never watched fail is not a guard".** The pipe-guard's command list was a hard-coded alternation carrying `check:context` but not `check:docs-structure`, making it **the one gate command whose exit code could be masked by `| tail`**. Replaced the literal with the character class `check:[a-z-]+`, so any future `check:*` is covered by construction. **Verified with a 10-case battery run against the new hook AND the committed old one side by side** (`git show HEAD:…` as the control): the target case flipped `old=0 → new=2` — proving the gap was real and is now closed — while all four other block-cases and all five must-not-block cases returned **identical** results on both versions, proving no regression and no new false positive. 10/10.
- 🔑 **The through-line of all three: the same defect, four homes.** `docs/CLAUDE.md:37` (skills), `:38` (hooks), `module-close/template.md:50`/`:62` (gate steps), `block-shell-dialect-mixup.sh:99` (gate steps again) were all **hard-coded enumerations of a moving environment**. Three were prose and went quietly wrong; the fourth was *executable* and silently stopped guarding. **Every one was replaced with a pointer or a pattern rather than a corrected count** — `discipline.md`'s rule, applied uniformly for the first time. ⚠️ **And the lesson the fourth one adds, which prose alone would not have taught: a stale enumeration inside a hook is not a documentation defect, it is a disabled guard.**
- **Process note.** Ishay asked *"ההערה שלך ל-7.7 תיעלם בשקט?"* about my plan to leave a correction inside the plan file. That question is what converted this from "leave a note for someone else" into "fix the permanent file and close the item", which also removed the double-fix risk. **Worth generalising: a correction parked in a mortal file is only as durable as the item it corrects.**

### 06/08/2026 23:55 — **Advisor-delegated task closed: `src/App.routes.test.jsx` now guards "new screen is open by default" (`src/CLAUDE.md`'s biggest-blast-radius gap). Real red proven, not just green. Gate green.**

Two more permanent tests live in the same file as a standing regression check: a synthetic unprotected-route tree (must flag) and a synthetic protected-parent-with-unwrapped-children tree (must NOT flag, proving the inheritance logic doesn't over-fire).
- **Enforcement:** no new CI step needed — `npm run test:run` already runs in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, so this test is enforced on every PR from today. `src/CLAUDE.md`'s "no lint, no test, no warning" line updated to reflect the guard now exists (with the red-proof evidence cited inline, per this repo's citation discipline).

- **Scope note:** this only covers `App.jsx`'s route-level gate. It does not, and was not asked to, cover column-level RLS gaps (`product_costs` split, already handled elsewhere) or menu-level `Sidebar` filtering (cosmetic layer, not a security boundary per `src/CLAUDE.md`'s own framing — "האכיפה האמיתית היא ב-DB").

### 06/08/2026 (analysis session) — **29 agents across four streams answered "why does spec→blueprint break while blueprint→build works". The method is not broken; five days of output contained six lines of product code. Plus one live customer-facing defect nobody had found.**
- 🔴 **THE LIVE DEFECT — the quote PDF's printed waterfall does not add up.** `src/lib/pricing.js:145-146` asserts *"סכום השורות המוצגות תמיד מתחבר לסה״כ המוצג"*; each row is rounded independently, so it does not. Verbatim, from the real engine: `1×06ST`(800) + `30×REG-TAG`(3), 5%, VAT 18% → PDF prints `890 ₪ / -45 ₪ / 846 ₪` (890−45=845). Measured on 4,480 catalog-realistic quotes: **38.5% of PDFs do not add up; 28.6% print a different discount on screen than in the PDF** (`Math.round(-44.5)=-44` at `QuoteSummaryPanel.jsx:44,54` vs `Math.round(44.5)=45` at `quotePdf.jsx:450-452`). It survived four audit rounds because the single binding acceptance case (6,300→6,319 ₪) divides exactly. **Also violates iron rule 14** — arithmetic in the view layer, two lines below a comment claiming there is none. Verified twice independently (adversary + defence, both re-ran the shipped formulas).

- 🔴 **BLOCK ①א RAN, AND EXECUTION FOUND THREE DEFECTS IN MY PLAN THAT READING IT NEVER DID:**
  - **The regex in 1.6 was wrong, and the reason is the interesting part:** `\d+ מתוך 8` matches BOTH *"מסך 4 מתוך 8"* (screen **number** 4) and *"4 מתוך 8 סגורים"* (4 **closed**) — **identical form, opposite meaning.** As written: **14 findings, 4 real — 71% false positives.** With a closing-word required on the same line: **4 findings, 4 real.** 🔑 **The lesson, and it is general: a check that produces false positives is a check people switch off — and that is worse than never building it, because afterwards the impression remains that it exists.**
  - **The verification command I wrote for 1.2 could never return 0.** It grepped all of `docs/`, and the forbidden string lives in the plan itself, in the dated journal, and in the card that quotes it as a banned form. **Narrowed to `docs/mockups/`.** **A verification that cannot pass is not a verification.**

- **Options verdict (3 designed independently, judged, then attacked):** the **minimal** option won 31–29–26. **The by-deliverable split — the closest design to Ishay's own "maybe three prompts" instinct — lost**, and for a measured reason: it raises the files a session must be told to read from 5 to 12+, defended by a maintained reading list, which is the exact artifact that already failed (`discovery_lessons.md:604-608`). **Discovery stays ONE prompt; what changes is the routing after it.** And the real two-prompt structure already exists and is not the Discovery split: an **opening** prompt (once) and a **continuation** prompt (`next-session-prompt.md`, every session after).

### 06/08/2026 (advisor #3, continued) — **A two-pass skill-scan of `src/CLAUDE.md` — one pass by the advisor, one by an independent fresh agent — and the agent caught two measurement errors in the advisor's own pass.**

- **`evaluation-first` inverts the plan's order** ([Anthropic](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/equipping-agents-for-the-real-world-with-agent-skills)): run without a skill → document failures → 3 scenarios → baseline → *then* minimal instructions. ⇒ **module 4 is the experiment's baseline arm, not practice**, and the prompt must NOT be trimmed mid-run.

### 06/08/2026 (advisor, second stretch) — **The success metric for the future Discovery skill turned out to be a single measurable number — and it is a finding about the system, not a compliment.**

- 🔴 **Measured across both sessions the same day: Ishay is the primary error-detection mechanism.** The new case table in `discovery_lessons.md §2` records **5 of 6** failures caught by him; in the parallel advisor shift, **4 of 4** advisor defects were his catches (an inflated "5 of 7" that was 4 · the skill never re-read after compaction · an invented three-skill split · a fix block written in line numbers when `CLAUDE.md` mandates grep anchors). ⇒ **The "how caught" column *is* the skill's spec** — every row saying "ישי" is a candidate to become a grep or a checklist line, and **the eval measures how many move to mechanical.** Written to `discovery_lessons.md §8`, not only to the board, on Ishay's correction: *"conclusions that aren't in the skill-build files won't be implemented."*
- **Evaluation-first, and it inverts the plan** ([Anthropic](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/equipping-agents-for-the-real-world-with-agent-skills)): evals → baseline → *minimal* skill → iterate. ⇒ **module 4's Discovery is the baseline arm of the experiment, not practice for it** — which also means **the 356-line prompt must NOT be trimmed mid-run**; minimality is a rule for the skill, and trimming the baseline contaminates it.
- **Handover-prompt review, 3 findings, all verified and all fixed** (`50035a9`): the sharpest was **the correction landing OUTSIDE the pasted block while the sentence it replaced stayed INSIDE it** — the file said both things and a reader obeys the one in the operative block · `screens-approved.md` had **0 mentions** in the handover while holding the answer to the handover's own acceptance-test question · and a surface count contradicting itself in three places.
- **Languages ruled (§7.87) — and Ishay's reasoning replaced Claude's.** Claude argued stale self-reported data; **Ishay argued the job**: *"maybe someone will be at the printer and doesn't need the language."* ⇒ one event has **different positions**, so an event-level gate assumes uniformity that does not exist. `speaks_english` is an info field on the hostess card — **not a layer-1 gate, not a score component, not a pool-table column** — which cost zero reopening because all three screens carrying it are still undesigned. 🔑 **Same insight as the deferred per-role headcount cap: an event is not homogeneous.**
- ➕ **`~/.claude/references/session-handoff-guide.md`** — cross-project, Hebrew, written for Ishay: when to rotate · compact vs fresh session · the four-part acceptance test · what belongs in a handover · and the contingency (make compaction harmless rather than prevent it). Every claim tagged 🌐 sourced / 📏 measured-here / 💭 inference.
- ⚠️ **Four measurement near-misses in one day, all nearly producing false findings:** PowerShell reading UTF-8 as ANSI · `Measure-Object -Line` skipping blank lines · `grep "אנגלית"` against a field named `speaks_english` · and a Hebrew grep returning 0 on a string sitting in a heading. ⇒ **an absence-claim is checked in at least two variants — the human's word and the code's.**
- **Broke: nothing.** Four commits, explicit pathspec each; a parallel session's uncommitted work sat in the tree throughout and none of it was swept.

### 06/08/2026 (advisor) — **A cold read of another session's file found four gaps its own author had not — and a compaction silently switched off three of the advisor's own mechanism rules.**

- **Cold-read of `docs/specs/module_04_hostesses/processes-approved.md` (293 lines)** by the advisor as a reader who was never in the Discovery conversation. That session had already fixed three gaps at Ishay's prompting; **four more surfaced**: the rating column's replacement name existed nowhere (only the ruling that it is *not* "דירוג") · no `פג תוקף` counter although §ב4 builds its whole ruling on that number being visible · the six statuses were scattered across five sections · and one chip carried no trigger threshold while its four neighbours all did. **All four closed by the Discovery session before it compacted** (`next-session-prompt.md` L62–65: "התרשמות" · split "ממתינות" · 8-week threshold · "אישרה זמינות"). 🔑 **The measured lesson: an author cannot certify their own document** — self-review found three only after being asked; a reader without the conversation found four more.
- 🔴 **`advisor` v13 → v14 (`2a8eaad`)** — re-read the skill after every **compaction**, not only when its rules move under a running shift. **Second occurrence of the same outcome via a new cause:** this shift crossed two compactions and kept advising while three *mechanism* rules quietly stopped running — the `⏸️` counter, "what was explicitly NOT checked" in a critique, and same-turn writes to a register — and again **only Ishay's explicit question exposed it** ("האם קראת שוב את סקיל היועץ?"). The discriminator added is the operative half: **what needs judgement survives a compaction; what needs remembering a detail does not — suspect the mechanism rules first.**
