- **② `screens-approved.md` appeared ZERO times in the handover** (measured) — the file that holds three approved cards and the six-section card structure. **Added to the read order as 4א, with the reason: mandatory before writing a new card, or you will contradict them.** *(This item only breaks at the moment of a turn, which is why it survived until now.)*
- **③ One stale `0 מתוך 9 משטחים` remained in the header** — now `3/8`. **④ The acceptance questions gained the languages ruling**, including a trap-check: *"if your answer is 'because English does not matter' — you read it wrong."*
- ✅ **⑤ Languages — RULED by Ishay after `research §7` item 8 sat tagged ⚪ *"לשאול את ישי"* and was never asked.** Reality: **the company does run events that require English.** **Decision: a boolean field on the hostess card only** (add popup · edit window · view card — **all three still undesigned ⇒ zero reopening of a closed screen**). 🚫 **Not a layer-1 gate, and not a pool-table column.** 🔴 **His reasoning, and it is the load-bearing part:** *"אולי סבבה מישהי שתהיה עם המדפסת ולא צריכה את השפה — אין מה להגביל."* ⇒ **one event holds different ROLES; an event-level gate assumes every position is identical, and that assumption is false.** Plus: **a hard gate on a self-declared field hides candidates silently**, while the manager picks manually anyway (C5:311) — **the algorithm ranks, she chooses.** 📌 **And it is the same insight as the open "headcount cap per role" item (Ishay, 29/07) — an event is not homogeneous, it has positions. One argument covers both; the positions model itself stays deferred.** **Written to:** `db_roadmap` (column) · `processes-approved §א1` (field row) · **`PROJECT_MASTER §7.87` (closed, with the reasoning)** · 🚧 next step if needed: **a `דוברת אנגלית` context chip on the candidate card, never a gate.**
- 🧪 **And from today, a new failure is recorded as a CASE, not a paragraph** — four columns: **input (quote or path+section) · what happened · what should have happened · who caught it.** **Ishay's reasoning: Anthropic is evaluation-first; module 4 is the experiment's base arm, and the valuable test cases are reproducible failure traces. A lesson cannot be run; a case can.** Six cases from today written in that format. ⚠️ **Old prose is not rewritten backwards** — the format applies going forward.

### 06/08/2026 (turn close) — **Handover prompt refreshed to 3/8, and the CORRECTED PROCESS written as a chapter at Ishay's request.**

- **Handover fixed:** `0/9` → **`3/8`** · nine surfaces → **eight** (1 and 3 are two tabs of one page) · the recommended order for the five that remain · **the new screen procedure written out in full** (draw first · HTML · the palette-grep command · the six sections · and the precedence rule) · **and the four acceptance questions replaced** with four whose answers live in the files written this turn.
- **The corrected process, written as `discovery_lessons.md §7` — and it is deliberately not the process that was planned, but the one left standing after measuring what worked:**
  **One process:** turn raw spec + raw mockups into **the single source of truth for the build**.
  **Six sub-processes, not seven** — *screen* and *mockup* merged.
  🔁 **And three loops that run ACROSS all of them rather than after them:** **write** (every ruling to disk the moment it is said; commit after every approved card) · **harvest** (rounds · **which prompt section never fired, by name** · where the prompt was wrong, quoted) · **verify** (every claim same-turn; every filter shown in full; an advisor or agent is **a recommendation to examine, never a conclusion**).
  🚪 **Four gates that do not move:** product/UX rulings are Ishay's · **never decide on a citation alone** (the test: *if all sources said the opposite, would my answer change? if yes — I quoted, I did not decide*) · **never manufacture openness and never close in silence** · no agent without approval.
- 🔑 **And the structural insight the whole turn produced: the process was assumed LINEAR and it is LOOPED — the three loops are what make it survive interruption.** *(Evidence: this session survived one compaction and ~15 commits without losing anything — not because anything was remembered, but because there was nothing left to remember.)*
- **Five measured lessons recorded:** ① **a rule Claude wrote itself is the rule Claude most often breaks** (three instances today) · ② **a blind spot is inherited through a reading list** — its author cannot list what they never read · ③ *"not built" ≠ "not decided"*, **and inverted: never manufacture openness** · ④ **`grep` does not read prose** — a debt inside another debt is unregistered · ⑤ **a picture beats text for BOTH readers** — Ishay understands it, and Claude reads HTML.

### 06/08/2026 — 🔴 **"אז אין חורים?" — there was one, and it sat on the single largest missing piece of the score.**

- **Ishay refused the reassurance and demanded the spec list every debt in detail** (*"אני יודע שזה מודול 6 אבל זה מאוד קשור — האפיון צריך לציין את כל החובות ולפרט מה בדיוק"*). **A registry scan found it.**
- 🔴 **The hole: nothing in `§6` told module 6 to FILL the attendance fields.** The reliability component — **weight `0.35`, the biggest component the score is missing** — depends on `attendance_status`/`lateness_level`/`no_show_reason` **and `assignments.actual_hours`**, all of which are written at the M6 operational close. **The only registered `🚧 מ6 ← מ4` was the three-state quality marking — which is the OTHER HALF OF THE SAME ROW ON THE SAME SCREEN.**
- ⚠️ **Why it escaped, and this is the transferable part:** `db_roadmap` assigns the **columns** to module **"4/8"**, not to 6; and *"`actual_hours` מתמלא בסגירה התפעולית של מ6"* appears **only inside the prose of a different debt** (`🚧 מ8 ← מ4`, the salary report). ⇒ **opening module 6 reads the registry via `grep '🚧 מ6'`, would have found only the quality marking, and would have built half a screen** — leaving reliability permanently off, **silently and correctly**, with nobody able to say why it never ended.
- 🔑 **Lesson recorded: a debt mentioned only inside another debt's prose is not registered. The registry is read by `grep`, and `grep` does not read prose.**
- ✅ **Written:** a new `🚧 מ6 ← מ4` carrying the ruled reliability values so M6 need not hunt for them (arrived `1.0` · slight `1.0` · moderate `0.75` · heavy `0.5` · `approval_withdrawn` `0.5` · no-show `0` · **sick/pre-approved excluded from the base entirely** · **and filter `project_status != 'cancelled'`**, or a client cancellation punishes an innocent hostess). **Plus chapter-12 item ⑪ — module 4's five debts in both directions with a status each**, so the map exists in the spec and not only in the registry.

### 06/08/2026 — 🔴 **Ishay pointed at one chip and it exposed the screen lying by implication. Screen 3 of 8 approved.**

- **His question, pointing at `עבדה אצל מדיטק 1×`:** *"מה זה עוזר אם היא עבדה? זה חלק מהאלגוריתם?"* **Answer: no.** The score is three components only (0.40 / 0.35 / 0.25); *"worked for this client"* is a **sort angle and an information column**, not a component. **But the mockup placed it on the same line as `אישרה 9 מ-10` and the distance — which ARE the score** ⇒ **a context chip read as a ranking reason.** 🔑 **The chips replace the score by saying *why*; a chip that is not a why makes the whole device dishonest.**
- ✅ **Fixed structurally, not cosmetically — two chip families:** **dark-bordered = the score itself in words** (`אישרה 9 מ-10` · `2.5 ק"מ`) · **grey = context and sort-angle inputs, not in the score** (`עבדה אצל <לקוח> 3×` · `יש רכב`) · **amber = attention** (`לא ענתה ל-4 האחרונים`). **And one special case recorded: `עבדה לאחרונה לפני X שבועות` IS in the algorithm — as the fairness lever's MULTIPLIER, not as a component.** **Full table written to chapter 12 as item ⑩**, because a builder reading the chips would otherwise implement "worked here 3×" as a score input in silence.
- 🔑 **And why it is not in the score, which is the part worth keeping:** the judgement about that client **already lives elsewhere — in the three-state per-client marking** (`מצוינת / בסדר / לא-לשלוח-שוב`), which is **layers 1 and 2, not layer 3.** **A count is not a judgement** (`research §6.11`: raw counts are a documented anti-pattern), **and the world does exactly this** — Ubeya's crown is a marker, not a score input.
- **Third mockup added on his request — two sort angles side by side** (`03_sortangles_DRAFT.html`): same five hostesses, **the order changes and the cards do not**, and **the angle never filters**. The teaching case is אורית — **4th by proximity** (furthest, and four unanswered invitations) and **1st by "worked for this client"** (5 times at מדיטק) — **and both are true at once, which is exactly why the angle exists: the manager decides what matters for THIS event, not the algorithm.**
- **Event-close inputs re-confirmed for him against `PROJECT_MASTER` (verified, not recalled):** per hostess, on one row — **① attendance** (§7.16א) → the reliability component (arrived=1.0 · slight=1.0 · moderate=0.75 · heavy=0.5 · no-show=0 · **sick/pre-approved excluded from the base entirely**) · **② the three-state per-client quality marking** → layers 1–2 (pin to top / gate out **for that client only**) · **③ `actual_hours`** → payroll (M8). **Both ① and ② are `🚧 מ6` and do not exist yet — which is why reliability is off and pinning is empty, and the screen says so out loud.**

### 06/08/2026 — **Smart Match mockup drawn; the research agent returned and one of its findings contradicts a ruling of ours.**

- 🔴 **The finding that matters most, and it must not be buried: our four named sort angles are a deviation from universal practice.** The agent searched and **found no product documenting two or more competing named ranking orders on a people list** — the universal pattern is **one ranked order plus many filters** (Sidekicker: a single *"Best match"* · LinkedIn: ~17 "Spotlights" which are **filters**, not sorts · Upwork, Indeed: filters). 🔑 **The defence, and it rests on our scale:** the world filters because it has thousands of candidates and must **narrow**; **we have 23 past the gate, the whole list fits on screen, and narrowing is pointless** ⇒ **re-ordering is the right tool at our size and filtering is the wrong one.** Recorded as a deliberate deviation, not quietly kept.
- ✅ **The hidden-score ruling has strong precedent — and the closest analogue does the opposite, which is what makes it a good conference answer.** Sidekicker ranks with the score **never printed** and a *"Recommended"* tag instead · LinkedIn Recruiter ranks with **no numeric score at all** · Greenhouse states outright *"AI doesn't score or rank applications"* · Instawork uses categorical buttons and tiers. **⚠️ But Ubeya — the nearest analogue — ships *"a single Match Score, showing next to the employee's name"*, colour-banded.** ⇒ **the answer at the conference is not "that's how it's done" but "there were two schools and we chose Sidekicker's over Ubeya's, because X".**
- 🏆 **And the chips have one near-twin: LinkedIn Recruiter's "Spotlights"** — `Active talent` (*"more likely to respond"*) and **`Missed candidates`** (*"uncontacted for longer than a week"*), which is almost exactly our *"עבדה לאחרונה לפני X שבועות"*; Ubeya's crown = *"worked with the client before"* = our *"עבדה אצל הלקוח הזה"*, as an icon with the reason in a tooltip. 🔴 **And the negative finding: no documented design-system pattern for per-row ranking-justification chips on people lists.**
- ⚠️ **Honesty correction to an earlier conference claim, made rather than left standing:** *"no product ranks by responsiveness"* **narrows** — LinkedIn does rank on *"more likely to respond"*, but that is **platform activity, not responsiveness to invitations we sent.** ⇒ the precise claim is **"no product ranks by acceptance rate of our own invitations"**. Still true, now defensible.
- **Ishay's standing rule, stated this turn:** *"קח יועצים חיצוניים בערבון מוגבל — אתה מחובר לקוד ולשיחה. קח את זה כהמלצה ובחן בעצמך, או תשאל. אל תניח."* **Already applied to this agent's output** — the responsiveness claim was narrowed rather than copied, the sort-angle finding was surfaced rather than absorbed, and marketing-sourced claims (SmartRecruiters, Bullhorn's "top 10") were kept flagged as vendor claims.
- 🎨 **And the mockup was too colourful — measured, not judged.** A count over `src/` shows **`slate` outnumbers every other colour roughly 5:1**: the real app is grey with a teal accent and rare status colours. **The first draft inverted that ratio** (tinted row backgrounds *and* coloured pills *and* three chip colours). **Fixed: row backgrounds removed, and a chip stating a good fact is no longer coloured — its text already says it.** ⇒ **one coloured element per row, not three.** *(This is the same rule Claude wrote two screens ago — "צבע = פעולה, לא קישוט" — and broke again. Third instance of "the rule Claude wrote itself is the one Claude breaks".)*

### 06/08/2026 — **The card structure settled: six sections beside the mockup, click-map first. And the Smart Match research agent dispatched on Ishay's approval.**

- **His question:** *"צריך לפני מוקאפ שתכתוב על המסכים פירוט? או שהמוקאפ והתהליך מספיקים — שלא יהיה בלבול מה כפתור ומה מוביל למה?"* **Answer: not *before*, but *beside* — and only what a picture cannot show.** 🔴 **Both alternatives were measured and failed on the same day:** *"detail first"* produced two verbal cards and *"מודה שלא כל כך הבנתי"*; *"mockup only"* produced *"זה לא כפתור נכון?"* — **the picture could not tell him.**
- **Six sections, and the click-map is first** (what is clickable · what it does · where it leads, one row per interactive element) **because button, link and bold text look alike, and the difference between *acts* and *routes* is a product decision.** Then: the single decision · a source for every number **including its time window** · states that are not on screen · permissions · and the definitions behind an on-screen word (*"דחוף = 72 שעות"*). 🚫 **And what is no longer written: layout, order, density, colour, emphasis — the mockup is the source and it governs**, otherwise a screen has two truths, which is exactly what the old mockups produced.
- 🔎 **Research agent dispatched (Ishay: *"ושלח"*) — one agent, one domain, ~70K**, scoped to the one genuinely open question for the Smart Match screen: **how do real products present a ranked list of people WITHOUT exposing the score, and what do they show instead** — plus reason-chip practice, named sort dimensions, list length, **and explicitly what it searched for and did not find.** Sent with §5's three rules (cited URL or the literal *"מהידע שלי, לא אומת"* · what the product does vs what marketing claims · exact on-screen wording over paraphrase). **Timing is deliberate: the answer lands before the mockup is drawn, not after** — so it does not cost an extra revision round.

### 06/08/2026 — ✅ **Screen 2 of 8 closed (2/8), and the process conclusions filed in the four places Ishay asked for.**

- **Ishay delegated both calls** (*"נותן לך להחליט על זה"*). **① Screen 2 closed** — mockup `git mv`'d to `approved/01_overview_approved.html`, and the card now states something it did not before: **the mockup is PART of the spec, not an illustration beside it — whatever it shows and the text omits, the mockup governs.** Without that line a screen has two truths.
- **② No research agent for this screen, with a reason rather than thrift:** every decision on it already rests on the code or on a prior ruling, and it has a documented world anchor (`research §2` — ASA's 120+ metrics are all fill metrics, none measures worker quality). **An agent would confirm, not discover.** 🔴 **Where the spend IS justified: before the Smart Match mockup** — that screen is the conference exhibit and carries the most invented UI, none of it checked against the world (reasoning chips instead of a score · four sort angles · eight visible candidates). **The question there is real and open: how do real products present a ranked candidate list without exposing the score — and if none does, that is itself the conference finding.**
- **③ Conclusions filed, at his request, in four places:** the harvest register gained a **ד+ה row** naming what never fired (`§7`'s front-end-design skill; **and that `§7` as a whole never says to read `src/`**) plus both of his quotes that caused the changes · **the new palette rule written there with the command itself** · `discovery_lessons.md` gained two "what worked" rows and **a new failure family (ו)**: verbal description instead of a mockup, and design language copied from the previous mockup.
- 🔑 **And the sharpest lesson of the day, now on disk: a rule Claude wrote itself is the rule Claude is most likely to break** — because it feels already known. **Both of today's last two failures were exactly that** (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `ishay_calibration.md §1` both say "for anything visual, show a mockup"; both were violated on two consecutive screens).

### 06/08/2026 — 🔴 **"תסתכל בקוד האמיתי" — and the code overturned Claude's colour answer within one turn.**

- **Ishay's instruction:** *"אולי אפילו תסתכל בקוד האמיתי, שיהיו צבעים אחידים ועקביים במערכת על אותו קו עיצוב — זה חשוב בבניית המוקאפים."* **He was right, and the answer given minutes earlier was half wrong.**

'bg-green-100 text-green-700' : 'bg-slate-200 text-slate-600'`, and the shared `RowAction.jsx` uses `tone==='approve'` → `border-green-200 bg-green-50 text-green-700` · **`red-600` = error** (access-denied, load failure) · amber = warning · **font is Geist Variable, not Segoe UI.**

It is not — green for approved is ALREADY the system's convention, in two places, one of them a shared component.** ✅ **His instinct was right and the mockup now follows the code.** **The half that survives — and now on evidence rather than taste: red must NOT mean "declined", because in this codebase red means *error*, and a refusal is a healthy outcome.**
- 🔑 **Method lesson, and it is the sharper one: a mockup's design language must be read from `src/`, not from the previous mockup.** The previous approved mockup was itself an approximation; copying it propagates drift. **Candidate rule for the harvest register: before drawing, grep the real palette — it costs one command and it caught two violations here** (an off-palette blue, and a wrong claim about what green means).

### 06/08/2026 — **Ishay's colour question, and a rule-8 violation Claude had just committed.**

- **He asked whether `אושרו` should be green and `סירבו` red — and answered himself in the same breath:** *"אני כן רוצה שיהיה בוגר אז אולי מיותר, מה מקובל בדרך כלל"*. **His instinct is right, and the reason is specific to this screen: colour here is ACTION, not decoration.** Red already carries two sharp meanings — *"short, inside 24 hours"* and *"the link is dead"*. **Make refusals red too and the manager learns to ignore red**, which is the one thing a triage screen cannot afford. **And a refusal is not a fault**: a hostess who said "can't" is a healthy outcome and nobody erred.
- **Nor green for approved:** the system **already has** a colour meaning *"this is the good thing"* — teal (rule 8, `PROJECT_MASTER §4`). A second green would be a third colour that means nothing else anywhere. ⇒ **the teal family becomes "progress toward filled"**: solid = arrived, light + **"אשר →"** = one click away.
- 🔴 **And checking it surfaced Claude's own violation: the mockup used `#0369A1` — a blue that is not in the approved palette.** Iron rule 8 forbids inventing colours. **Replaced with teal.** *(Caught only because he asked about colour at all; a palette check is not currently part of the mockup step — candidate for the harvest register.)*

### 06/08/2026 — **Two more handover rules Ishay caught as missing — and together they change the goal from "avoid compaction" to "make compaction harmless".**

- **④ Commit after every APPROVED CARD, not "at the end of each block".** *"Block" is undefined, and auto-compaction fires with no warning around 80%.* 🔑 **A committed working tree is a full restore point — that is the entire fallback plan: not to prevent the compaction, but to make it harmless.** *(Explicit pathspec always; never `git add -A`.)*
- **⑤ If auto-compaction fires mid-task — do NOT continue. Two actions first, in order:** `git status` + `git diff`, **because that is exactly the information a compaction erases** (the documented failure being *"the agent did not know which files had already been edited"*) — **and then run the acceptance test on yourself, which is precisely what it exists for.** Only after both, back to work: **carrying on as if nothing happened is how a half-step gets erased silently.**
- 🔑 **Why these two matter more than they look:** the acceptance test protects the *incoming* session; ④ and ⑤ protect the *interrupted* one. **Together they close the loop — the failure mode this session already paid for twice** *(a turn dying between announcing an action and doing it; the resumed turn acting as if the announced step happened)*.

### 06/08/2026 — **Screen 2 presented, and the handover prompt gained an ACCEPTANCE TEST — because the old one "worked half".**

- 🔴 **Ishay's diagnosis, and it is the sharpest process finding of the day: a reading list says WHICH FILES TO OPEN, not WHAT YOU MUST BE ABLE TO ANSWER.** Measured consequence: **both big post-compact catches — the full `C5 §5.6.10–5.6.12` and `research §1–§9` — came from Ishay, not from the prompt.** ⇒ `next-session-prompt.md` now opens with a four-part acceptance test the incoming session answers **before any action**: 🅐 mechanical (open every path named here; a dead path halts — *the check that would have caught line 35 the moment `ishay_calibration.md` moved*; plus `git status`/branch match) · 🅑 survivability (every decision, path and blocker tagged *"from file X"* or *"from memory"*) · 🅒 judgement (do the blockers still exist; do the *"לאישורך"* assumptions still hold) · 🅓 🔴 **four factual questions the outgoing session writes, answerable only from what it just wrote to disk** — **which is what converts the list from *what to open* into *what you must know*: if the answer is not in the files we named, read more and say what the list is missing.** *(Form adopted from `softaworks/agent-toolkit` + Claude Code docs — adopted, not invented.)*
- **Two more additions:** a **`Continues from:`** line at the head of every handover, so the files form a chain rather than a disconnected series · and a **🔴 turn line at 640K context** — finish the current card, commit, stop; **never start a new card above the line, and never compact — a fresh session plus the test.** *(Ishay watches the number; Claude carries the threshold.)* **Plus one line per turn into `discovery_lessons.md §5ג` recording what the test actually caught** — without it the design stays speculative; with it there is data after 3–4 turns.
- ✅ **Screen 2 (מבט-על שיבוץ) presented — and its single decision is Ishay's own, cited to a FILE rather than to the chat** (`discovery-log.md`, the transcript-extraction section, item 2), on his explicit instruction: *"אחרי הסיבוב הבא הצ'אט הזה לא קיים"*. **Three holes found in that mockup, all the same family — two opposite actions rendered identical:** 🔴 **the four counters have no cell for `אישרה זמינות`**, so an event where six confirmed and none were finalised shows `אושרו 0` and looks identical to one nobody answered — *one needs her to click approve, the other needs more invitations* · `ממתינות` must split off its expired part · and **`חוסרים קריטיים` is defined wrong for triage** (5-of-6 tomorrow beats 0-of-2 next month) ⇒ **critical = still short inside T-24**, which is the alarm already ruled in §ב7 rather than a new definition. **Also: the global Smart Match button drops — C5 §5.6.10 rules that entry is a row click**, and a global button with no event selected leads nowhere.

### 06/08/2026 (final) — **Ishay offered a full read of the research doc "for safety". It was not ceremony: it corrected a ruling's reasoning and surfaced two items the document had explicitly addressed to him that nobody ever delivered.**

- **Why the offer was accepted, stated plainly:** Claude had cited **§6.10** as the basis for ruling ⑥ **without having read it** — a citation with no same-turn check, which the global rules forbid. §1–§9 had never been read this session; only §3.2, §5-layer-4 and §11.
- ✅ **Ruling ⑥ gained independent, sourced confirmation Claude did not have when deciding:** **§6.7 lists every world precedent for this lever — Connecteam *"מספר משמרות שווה"*, Deputy *"פיזור שעות שווה"*, Skedulo *"Balanced workload"*, Nowsta *"שעות מתוכננות השבוע"* — 🔑 and every one of them measures WORK. Not one measures invitations.** (§9.5's sub-ruling table says the same of the quarter column: *"Nowsta מציגה שעות מתוכננות השבוע לשם כך"*.)
- 🔴 **Ruling ⑦ stands but its REASONING was wrong and is corrected.** Claude argued *"the pool is empty, nobody knows whether 4 is rare or routine"* — i.e. it leaned on learning-from-use. **§9.7 forbids exactly that: *"בפרויקט הזה אסור לנסח המלצות בנוסח 'לאמת מול הצעה אמיתית' — אין לקוח. הסבירות של פרמטר נמדדת מול עקביות פנימית ומול האפיון הקפוא, וזה הכול."*** There will be no use to learn from. **Correct reason: nine other thresholds already live in `params`, and the cost is zero.** *(Kept as a worked example — the ruling survived, the argument did not, and only reading caught it.)*
- ➕ **And a real gap the read exposed:** the chip covers **4 and up**; a hostess with **2 invitations and no answers gets no chip and stays invisible**, though she is equally dead. ⇒ below the threshold, show `לא ענתה מעולם (N זימונים)` — the same pattern as `טרם נצבר מידע` in the score. *(Checked and rejected as a concern: §6.11's raw-count anti-pattern does **not** bite here — the chip is a **streak**, not a rate over a small denominator.)*
- 🛑 **Two items the research doc addressed to Ishay and nobody delivered — zero mentions across all five files in the spec folder, measured:** **(1) Languages** — §7's missing-data table, item 8, is literally tagged **⚪ *"לשאול את ישי"*** and notes *"המוקאפ עצמו מדגים 'כנס בינלאומי'"* (`01.png` shows `כנס בינלאומי · חברת הייטק גלובל`). **Never asked.** If some events need English it is a **gate**, like the no-car-over-40km rule — and the field does not exist. *(The same §7 explicitly rejects certifications and appearance attributes; languages were left as a question, not rejected.)* **(2) The headcount cap — Ishay's own product insight, never carried forward:** §9.7 records him (29/07 23:58) proposing `min(ceil(guests ÷ ratio), cap)`, and the document itself names the hole: **the cap belongs to the ROLE, not the event** — six at a registration desk is sane, but a large event also needs ushers, cloakroom and multi-entrance direction, which are different positions, so a per-event cap **blocks legitimate staffing**. It touches **§7.67** (`assignments` links to `project_id` only, with no service-line attribution) **and already-built module-3 code**, and was parked as *"פריט להכרעה בסבב-קדם-מ4"* — **which is this session.**

### 06/08/2026 (end of block) — **The two flagged uncertainties were searched, found never ruled, and closed by Claude on Ishay's explicit delegation.**

- **Search performed before deciding** (Ishay offered the recorded transcripts): the research doc, §7, and **102 transcript files / 345 MB**. `שבועות_מאז_הזמנה_אחרונה` appears **18 times — every one a quotation of the same §11.2 line**, never a discussion. The chip's "4" appears exactly once outside this session, and that hit is **a read of `processes-approved.md` itself**. ⇒ **neither was ever ruled; both were genuinely open.**
- 🔴 **Ruling ⑥ — the fairness lever is measured from when she last WORKED, not when she was last INVITED.** Source: `max(projects.final_event_date)` — §3.2 is the only place that names the actual data source, and §11.5 displays *"עבדה לאחרונה"*; §11.2's `שבועות_מאז_הזמנה_אחרונה` is a wording slip and **must not be implemented as written**. **The argument on the merits, which is what decided it:** keyed on *invitation*, a hostess invited four times and never chosen **resets to "fresh" and loses the bonus — precisely the person the lever exists for**, and the manager would be silently killing bonuses every send-round. Only "worked" serves §6.10's rich-get-richer correction.
- **Ruling ⑦ — the chip's threshold lives in `params` (default 4), not hardcoded.** Not an appeal to consistency: **the pool is empty (0 rows, measured), so nobody knows whether four unanswered invitations is rare or routine.** A number we cannot know must not be a number only a developer can change — and nine other thresholds already sit in `params`.

### 06/08/2026 (later still) — 🔴 **Ishay pushed back on finding ①, and he was right about something larger than the label: the framing was wrong, and the reusable prompt is why.**

- **The error, stated plainly: Claude made *"does it match the frozen spec?"* the question** — in a session whose founding premise is that the frozen spec is **not** reliable (*"נכתב על ידי שאין לי ידע מספיק רחב בעולם הזה"*). "C6 agrees with us, therefore not a deviation" is **the same deference in better clothes**; and the mockups are grade 3 with known-wrong details, so "the mockups agree too" adds nothing. **The correct argument was already in hand and then buried under the citations: `אישרה זמינות` is right because it names WHO ACTS.** That holds even if all three sources said otherwise — and then it would be a documented departure. **A product judgement was turned into a citation contest.**
- 🔴 **And there IS a mockup departure that was missed, because ONE label was checked and the topic declared closed — the right unit of check was the whole list.** `03.png` names the first state **`נשלח מייל תיאום`** — i.e. by *our action*, where C6 names it by *her state* (`ממתינה למענה`): **the mockup commits the same class of error as C5, in the opposite direction.** It also **silently omits `שוחררה`** (present in C5, C6 and our six) and has no `approval_withdrawn`, which must be manually settable since it is a manager action. 🔑 **The real lesson: none of the three sources is authoritative here — C5 errs one way, the mockup the other, C6 happens to be right. So the ruling must come from "what is true on the screen", never from "who said what".**
- 📏 **Measured root cause, and it is in the artifact the future skill will be based on:** `prompt_module_discovery.md` **never carries the session's founding premise at all.** The word "הישן" about the spec appears **once, at line 24 — inside the block explicitly marked *"אתה קורא את זה, לא קלוד"***. Meanwhile the prompt instructs Claude to measure against the frozen spec **twice**: chapter 10 (*"סטיות מהאפיון הקפוא"*) and the reverse coverage check (*"חלץ מהאפיון הקפוא ומהמוקאפים את רשימת-הנדרש"*). ⇒ **the prompt installs the frozen spec as the baseline one justifies departures from — the exact inverse of what the session exists for.** A §0 premise block + a rename of chapter 10 (*"מה שונה… ולמה, לגופו של עניין"* — "סטייה" presumes the source is right) were drafted verbatim and are **awaiting Ishay's approval, not written.**
- ✅ **Ishay ruled on the `responded_at` gap — *"חשוב להתייחס לזה באפיון"*** ⇒ written as chapter-12 item ⑨ with the full chain (§11.7 makes *"תענה הכי מהר"* the default **under 72h** · the angle needs `responded_at − invite_sent_at` · the column does not exist), **plus two implementation traps recorded now rather than discovered later:** it must **never** be derived from `updated_at` (a trigger overwrites it, and M8 writes `salary_report_id` to the row months after the answer — forging every historical response time at once), and it is written **once, on first answer**, with resend refreshing `invite_sent_at` only, or a reminder round yields negative response times.

### 06/08/2026 (later) — **Post-compact self-audit, at Ishay's request — the measurement that will shape the end-to-end skill.**

- **What actually arrived after the compact, four sources:** the auto-summary (9 sections, ~34 of Ishay's messages verbatim, descriptions of 5 files, an errors-and-fixes list) · the SessionStart hook (STATUS's whole "עודכן לאחרונה" line + branch + uncommitted count + deadline + active plan) · the three `CLAUDE.md` files · the memory index. Plus three *"file was read before summarization but too large to include"* notices (`CLAUDE_CODE_LOG`, `STATUS`, `processes-approved`). **Not carried:** the content of all five spec-folder files, the mockups, C5/C6, the schema, the reusable prompt — **all re-read from disk this session.**
- 🔴 **Finding 1 — the compact carried a WRONG fact with perfect fidelity.** The summary stated ruling ⑤ as *"deviation from C5 5.5.8"*, exactly as the pre-compact session had written it. It was wrong (C6 and both mockups say `אישרה זמינות`). **A compact does not filter errors — it reproduces them with the same confidence as the truths.** What corrected it was re-reading the source; nothing that passed through the compact could have.
- 🔑 **Finding 2 — the operational reliance on the summary was ZERO.** Every fact needed to do the work was on disk, reached via `next-session-prompt.md`. The summary's *unique* content (verbatim messages, the errors list) is already durably held by `ishay-calibration.md` and this log. ⇒ **the more complete the on-disk handoff, the more redundant the summary becomes — which is the goal, because the summary is the one artifact that cannot be audited.**
- **Finding 3 — the compact instruction Claude drafted was not the one Ishay typed** (drafted: *"שמור: המצב והמצביעים. אל תשמור את מהלך השיחה"*; typed: *"שמור כל דבר נוסף שנראה לך חשוב"*). Result: a broad summary instead of a narrow one. **Not a mistake — a mechanism gap:** a drafted instruction that lives in chat has to be re-composed from memory at the moment of use. ⇒ **the compact instruction belongs in `next-session-prompt.md` as a copy-paste block**, the same way the opening prompt does. Recorded for the skill.
- **Ishay's three lines for the compact transition written into `prompt_module_discovery.md` §"מה ללמוד"** as items 4–6 (self-handoff = a one-line pointer, never a paste · the first post-compact message answers "is the task clear?" so compact loss surfaces in line one · and the caveat that `next-session-prompt.md`'s **content** is module-4-only — a future skill inherits its shape, not its text).

### 05/08/2026 18:3X — **The first live `advisor` shift. It drove the skill from v6 to v13, and every one of the seven rules that landed came from a failure measured inside the shift itself — not from design.**

The skill *points* at §1 and I read that pointer; I never applied it. Two concrete misfilings fell out: the login-spacing item is the **first screen of the conference hour** (filter 1), and the response-dictionary item was perishable. The classification was deleted as an invention. **A single occurrence stays a dated note; the shift's own failure did not become a rule.**
- 🔴 **Three claims reached Ishay this shift without a measurement behind them, and all three were the same species: plausible, inherited or inferred, unchecked.** ① *"the global playbook exists (176 lines) **without** the 8 dictionary rows"* — I verified existence and line count and **inferred the absence**; measured, **5 of 8 were already there**, so the item was 2–3 rows, not 8. ② the invented filter classification above. ③ 🔴 the costliest: **"the ~20 unauthenticated MCP servers block schema checks and forced the PR #11 merge through browser-Claude"** — inherited verbatim from board item ⏸️9 (written by another session, itself an inference) and repeated three times to Ishay and three times to the skill session. **Measured when he finally asked what the problem was: `list_projects` returns `Reg-In` / `yfeovxppnfoafmfbdfvh` / PG 17.6.1, `list_tables` returns 20 tables with live row counts, and `gh auth status` is logged in with `repo`+`workflow`.** Root cause: the session-start list names `supabase` and `plugin:supabase:supabase` — **a third, UUID-named Supabase server was authenticated the whole time**, and `gh` never needed the plugin. ⇒ ⏸️9 dropped from blocker to nice-to-have; ⏸️4 was never blocked; **the browser-merge stays correct as a rule of the skill, not as a capability limit** — a distinction that changes the reason the advisor gives Ishay. **v13 (`d57ef6a`): the board is context, not truth — a ⏸️ item that blocks or orders work is verified before you plan around it.** `## Init` already said that about the journal and said nothing about the board.
- **`docs/` was measured, and the metric I was proud of produced 8 false positives out of 9.** 67 `.md` / **19,411 lines**, each cross-referenced against **355 text files** repo-wide. **Exactly one orphan** (`archive/toolbox_catalog_2026-07.md`, 257 lines, 0 inbound) — and it does not want deleting, it wants **one pointer line from `docs/toolbox.md`**, the pattern `CLAUDE.md` already uses. The other eight flagged files were `archive/*_full_*` snapshots (few refs **by design** — and the archive proved itself a working restore point today: `PROJECT_MASTER:76` carries *"ארבעת החלקים … הוצל מהארכיון 05/08"*) and two `guides/reference/` files that are run **once per new machine**. 🔴 **The lesson about the instrument: inbound-reference count flags "rarely needed by nature" as "at risk".** And Ishay's later clarification reframed the whole job — his goal was never loss, it was **usability**: *"הם ארוכים וקשה לעשות בהם שימוש… מה שלא רלוונטי להעביר לארכיון"*. That clause was on the board in his own words (*"לעדכן/לשכתב/לארכב/למחוק"*) from the start; I built the measurement on the secondary caveat instead.

Verdict: do not rewrite it — 11 factual claims verified ✓ and the defects are in the files it points at.** Verified: 6 PNGs `01–06` in `hostesses-screen`; `quote-screen/08_*_approved.html`+`09_*`; **43 PNG vs 5 HTML exactly**; `check-docs-updated.sh:41` is precisely the read-only exemption; `smart_match_research` §9+§11 exist; all three prescribed greps work (§7→19 מ4 items · `🚧 מ4`→9 rows · `· מודול 4`→§5.10/5.11/5.12); `session-start-context.sh:33` quoted verbatim; `module_04_hostesses.md` §⑥1 carries the context-packet params. **Three defects:** 🔴 **`§6:394` still carries a live `🚧 מ4` token on the blueprint-path debt that `§6:393` declares closed (`5151c6b`) — and the prompt sends the session to read exactly that row *"לפני שאתה מתחיל"*, so it would pay a paid debt.** The irony is complete: **393's own lesson text describes this failure verbatim.** The fix wrote the lesson and left the trap. Plus: the prompt says module 3 had **7** PNGs (measured: **8**) inside a paragraph that orders self-verification; and `§6:397`'s journal measurement reads **1,141** against today's **1,383**. ⚠️ **Only the facts lens ran — the "what does it not mention" lens did not**, and by the dosing rule a prompt driving a massive session earns two.
- 🔴 **The 🚧 registry is enforced in one direction only, and that is why paid debts keep reading as open — fourth occurrence, so it earned a rule.** Iron rule 15 forces *guide token ⇒ §6 line*, which catches a **silent** debt and cannot catch a **paid** one. Ishay's own question found the instance: modules 1–2 were swept for closable debt, module 1 came back clean (all tokens point at unbuilt M9/M10/M12), and **module 2 carried `🚧 מ3` on `totalRevenue` — work delivered in step 3.5 and verified in code** (`customers.js:215` cites the M3 pricing SSOT; `customers.test.js` asserts 1,180 / 9,864.8 / frozen 17% VAT / "empty is not 0"). §6 itself was **clean** — all five מ3 rows struck with dates — so module 3's close swept the registry and left the **guide** and the **`src/` comment**. Landed in `module-close/template.md` §7: **the reverse sweep must PRINT its count** across four surfaces (§6 · micro-guides · `src/` comments · design notes), *"K may be 0 — say so"*. **A blocking hook was considered and rejected**: it would have to parse `STATUS.md`'s prose module table, and a false block teaches sessions to route around hooks — the principle `block-shell-dialect-mixup.sh` states about itself. Same medicine as the 🗡️ fix earlier today: **a sweep with no output is indistinguishable from a sweep that never ran, and Ishay is the only gate.**

A fresh checker (method-only, "אין ממצאים" named a complete answer) read all 14 archived files whole and returned **one** candidate — `builders.md:146–151`, the dead-session-with-uncommitted-work procedure — **and flagged its own weakness: the trigger has never fired here**, so it is Ishay's reality-filter call. Everything else was already rescued, and the checker caught itself nearly inventing a finding by grepping Hebrew against English skill files. 🔴 **He overrode me on `micro_guides/module-3.md`: I recommended archiving it (2,273 lines, 72% an append-only §9 the compaction rule forbids compressing), he ruled not to, and he was right for a measurable reason** — the guides still carry live debt tokens, so archiving would have frozen a stale one in place. His call stands whole.
- **Two rules of his own about how the advisor works.** *"אחרי שאישרתי תוכנית אתה יכול לשלוח כבר פרומפט למשימה הראשונה"* → v8 (`cef2afe`): an approved plan **is** the go-ahead for its first item. And *"תדבר איתי מהסקיל ולא מהזיכרון — בסוף אני מודד את הסקיל"* → v12: **re-read the file before quoting it when another session holds the writing chair.** That request is what exposed the shift running on v6 while v7–v8 landed underneath it; without it I would have reported my own two recommendations as "pending" when they were already at lines 100–107 and 170–178. ⚠️ **And the counter that proves the point: `⏸️ N פתוח` sat frozen at 11 through seven consecutive messages, including the one reporting three items ruled.** The skill defined what the counter counts and never when to recompute it. **Finally: his correction to the role — an advisor hands Ishay prompts for other sessions to do the dirty work, which also tests the advisor's prompts.** The whole shift was executed in-house; the `src/lib/customers.js` comment edit (the one surviving stale token) was handed over as a prompt instead, since `src/**` is a hard boundary for the role. 🔴 **And Ishay's reaction to that prompt is the sharper lesson — he laughed: *"סשן בשביל שורה? הלכת רחוק"*. He was right, and the skill's own boundary line offers TWO routes — *"a prompt for a build session, **or a ⏸️ item for Ishay**"* — and I took the heavy one for a one-line comment because he had said "from the next task, a prompt". Literal compliance breaking intent, which is the exact failure I had measured in others twice the same day.**
- 🔴 **His last recommendation of the shift found the largest single gap in the Discovery prompt, and it took him one sentence: *"ממליץ להגיד בפרומפטים מתי הם צריכים לקמט"*.** Measured immediately: `_shared/writing-prompts.md` had **zero** occurrences of commit/pathspec across the whole file — iron rule 10 says *how* (`git commit -- <paths>`, never `git add -A`) and **no prompt had ever said *when***. The handed-over prompt had just demonstrated it: correct edit, green gate, clean report, **and the edit left uncommitted**, so Ishay had to be told to tell the worker to commit. **And in the Discovery prompt it is far worse: stage ז writes up to SIX files** (the spec · the discovery log · `§7` write-back · `STATUS` · the journal · the step guide) **and carried no commit instruction at all** ⇒ six dangling files for the next session to either mis-own or sweep. Landed as **`writing-prompts.md` rule 19** (the moment · the explicit paths · push-or-not) and **step 12 in stage ז**.
- **Also added at his explicit request, after he asked whether the market-practice principle was in there: it was — substantially — but only half of it.** The "בדיקת פרקטיקות מקובלות" section already demanded a real search with a cited source (*"חיפוש בפועל, לא מהזיכרון… אם לא חיפשת, כתוב 'מהידע שלי, לא אומת'"*) and forbade using world practice to override his product intent; §0 itself flags that section as the best part of the prompt. **Missing was §1's two-part answer** — *"this is the convention **and it fits here because X**"* **or, stronger, "this is the convention **and I deliberately deviated, because X**"*. Without the second half the prompt steers toward adoption only, and a convention adopted without a fit-check is copying, not deciding — which is exactly what gets challenged in the conference hour.
- **Budget discipline at the close: Ishay approved up to 3 reviewers and I sent 1.** Measured why: the file's own line 6 records that **five** fresh reviewers already covered facts · feasibility · cross-stage contradictions · what-is-missing · exaggeration on this prompt, so two of my three proposed lenses would have returned findings already sitting in §0. **The one lens never run is "walk it as Ishay"** — where would he be asked to invent an answer, where would a round exceed 3 questions, where would he say "לא הבנתי", where would he approve without understanding. That is also precisely the outcome he named. ~70K instead of ~210K. **An approved budget is not a budget that must be spent.**
- 🔴 **That one agent returned 9 findings on a prompt five reviewers had already read — which means my own "approved as-is" verdict was too generous. I checked its facts; I never checked the conversation.** One root cause explains four of them: **the prompt never tells the session to read `PROJECT_MASTER §1`** — it cites it once, parenthetically, for a single rule, so §1's declaration-vs-question distinction and its winning conference answer never reach the session. The three severe ones: **① the "up to 3 questions" ceiling caps quantity, not TYPE** — three *process* questions ("how do you handle a hostess who cancelled?") comply fully and force Ishay, a student, to invent answers, while §1 says process arrives as a declaration inviting correction; and stage ב renders the flow as an **arrow diagram**, i.e. an abstract declaration, which earns a nod and lets a wrong model survive to the build. **② No screen is justified by its decision-maker and the one decision made on it** — stage ג asks for a *content enumeration* ("מידע, סינונים, טפסים, כפתורים"), the exact framing §1 rejects, so the sentence that wins the "too much information?" question at the conference is never produced. **③ The world-practice check fires on Claude's doubt, not on Ishay's not-knowing** — it runs only on "a real gap or doubt", so a process the spec makes look reasonable gets no comparison and he never learns there was something to compare. That is the thing he named as needing most. The other six: stage ו bundles 11 sections into one approval **of which three are decisions born inside that document** (scope boundaries · definition-of-working · what the blueprint may not guess) at the tired end of a long session, where the minimum-resistance path is "מאשר" · a seven-column table in Hebrew chat and **no mid-session progress indicator** (the close-the-loop table fires only at stage ז, so nothing supports "מספיק להיום") · the `/רעיונות-לבדיקה` column exists in stage ב and **vanishes in stage ו** · Ishay is asked to approve a **file path**, contradicting the prompt's own "don't ask me about file structure" · `"מאומת בפועל ✓"` carries no source, i.e. confidence dressed as measurement · and *"אין צורך במוקאפ חדש… **והמשך**"* turns a recommendation into a verdict in the same breath. **The lens-diversity rule earned itself here: 5 agents on other lenses found none of these.**

Ishay's generalization became the rule: **name the session TYPE first, then derive the reading rule from what it must produce**, on five axes — ① the reading layer (the *why* vs the *what*) · ② who releases each stop-point · ③ output shape · ④ what must never be handed over · ⑤ the commit moment (rule 19, landed the same evening) — with the cheap test *"whose reader is this addressed to?"*. ### 05/08/2026 01:4X — **The new deno CI gate failed on its first run ever, and its own comment had the cause backwards**
- `edge-function-check` (added in `c14bf32`, **this branch only** — never green) died in ~9s on
  `Could not find a matching package for 'npm:@supabase/realtime-js@2.112.0' in the node_modules directory`.
- **The comment above the job said skipping `npm ci` prevents exactly this.** It is inverted, and the
  inversion caused the failure. Per the Deno 2 CLI reference, what switches Deno into node_modules
  resolution is **the presence of `package.json` at the root**, not the presence of the directory: with a
  `package.json` and no `deno.json` the default is `nodeModulesDir: "manual"` = *use the existing directory,
  do not create it*. Removing `npm ci` made the failure **certain**, not impossible.
- Fixed with the documented flag — `deno check --node-modules-dir=none …` (*resolve npm from the global
  cache*), which is the behaviour the comment believed it was getting free, and the model the real Supabase
  Edge runtime uses. Comment rewritten so the next reader does not inherit the wrong cause.
- ⚠️ **Boundary: deno is not installed here, so the fix was not reproduced locally** — the evidence is
  documented flag semantics matching the error text exactly. Fallback if CI stays red: pull the job out of
  the M3 PR and re-land it separately; a permanently-red gate on `dev` trains people to ignore red.
- 🚧 מ10 recorded in the comment: the function imports `jsr:@supabase/supabase-js@2` (open major), so a
  future JSR publish can break this job **by itself**. Not changed now — product code, mid-merge.
- Untouched deliberately: branch protection. The PR says "Able to merge" while the check is red, i.e. it is
  not a required status check — Ishay's setting, not mine.

### 04/08/2026 — `skill-scan` + the failure-modes file, rescued from the manager corpus before it is archived
- **New skill `.claude/skills/skill-scan/`** — scans an instruction artifact (a skill, `CLAUDE.md`,
  a micro-guide) for rules that cannot fire, whose skip would be invisible, that do not earn their
  lines, or that are filed where their reader never goes. Five lenses, three brakes, an explicit
  wrong-home pass, a mandatory subtraction quota, and a hard rule that it never edits what it scans.
- **New `.claude/skills/_shared/failure-modes.md`** — the five structural failure modes measured
  across two arenas 01–02/08, plus the six self-review questions that measurably produced findings.
  Extracted from `work-manager/references/miss-ledger.md` because nobody opens 582 archived lines.
- **Why now:** the ledger's own dominant finding is that four consecutive shifts failed on a rule
  nobody ran, never on a missing rule — which is exactly what lenses 1 and 2 test for.
- **Broke nothing:** both files are new, no existing file edited, zero touches to `src/`, `e2e/`,
  migrations or the DB. 🚧 Not yet validated against a real fix outcome — every finding the scan
  emits carries "how will we know the fix worked" for that reason.
- **Validated by 9 paired eval runs over 2 iterations** — the same three requests answered with the
  skill and without it. **Round 1: won 2, lost 1. Round 2: won 3.** The wins that mattered: the
  harvest gate caught two live rules buried inside a block the baseline recommended deleting, and a
  genuinely dead rule (a checklist still routing writes to the CHANGELOG frozen 23/07).
  🔴 **The loss taught more than the wins** — the baseline read the file's own `git log` and checked
  the artifacts the file had produced; both methods were missing and are now in, together with an
  inverse test for overfitting and a check for rules that hand a reader an expected number instead
  of a method. **The scan then caught its own author with that second defect** (the skill carried
  measured figures as anchors) — figures removed, method kept.
- 🔴 **Six of those defects are now real debt, not prose** — two `🚧 מ4` lines in `PROJECT_MASTER §6`,
  the one registry a module opening greps by itself. Ishay's point, and it was the right one: a
  finding parked in a scratchpad is a finding that will not happen. Each line carries how we will
  know it was fixed. **Verified the mechanism fires:** `grep '🚧 מ4'` returns 5.
  ↳ **Two corrections the same night, both his.** (1) One registered line claimed `regin-docs-sync`
  "does not exist" — **wrong**: it is a scheduled routine documented in `docs/claude_routines.md`
  with a live copy under `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/`. I searched for a skill directory and
  concluded absence — an absence-claim narrower than its conclusion, third occurrence today and the
  only one that reached a permanent file. **The correction is stated inside the line itself**, so a
  future reader cannot inherit the false version. The residual defect is real but smaller: only
  `module-blueprint` phrases it "Claude runs it" against rule 13(ז). (2) Three findings had been
  parked as candidates under the graduation bar — **he asked who would be counting occurrences once
  the manager is archived. Nobody.** The bar presupposes the miss-ledger, so "candidate" now means
  "gone"; applying a rule without checking its precondition still holds is the instrument defect
  🅴 names. All three registered on their own merit; a fourth (unverifiable relayed approval) was
  deliberately **not** registered — it exists only while a manager relays, and dies with the role.
- **`docs/toolbox.md`:** `skill-scan` registered (the catalogue a session is told to read before
  proposing a tool did not list it — a dead catalogue), plus a new section giving the exact sentences
  for scanning, paired-eval testing and improving a skill, with the measured numbers behind them.
- 🔴 **`skill-scan` was then run against `CLAUDE.md` itself, by a fresh agent, with my prediction
  written down first — and the prediction was wrong 4 times out of 5**, which is the point of the
  tool. Two findings matter and await Ishay: **(a) `gitleaks` never runs on the branch he works on**
  — `ci.yml` fires it only on PR/push to `dev`/`main`, and the local pre-commit runs eslint+prettier
  only, so a secret committed to a personal branch reaches GitHub and stays in history; the DoD box
  is still sound because it asks about *merge*, but `CLAUDE.md`'s claim that the mechanism replaced
  the old "never commit secrets" rule is **narrower than what it replaced**. **(b) "a repeated
  question ⇒ fix the source document" cannot ever fire** — a fresh session has no cross-session
  memory and cannot know a question repeated; only Ishay can. Also recorded: a live contradiction on
  journal-entry length between this file and `CLAUDE.md`, and a wrong rule count in its header.
  **What the scan verified positively:** the 28/07 pruning cut 69% in one commit, the archive is
  byte-identical to the pre-pruning version, and 8 of 10 relocation claims resolve in their target

- 🔴 **Live defects surfaced and re-verified here, queued not fixed:** `regin-docs-sync` is cited as

  while the rule requires §6 (line 512 carries `🚧 מ9` inside §7, so it passes green on an empty

  personas measurably *damage* factual accuracy (the model optimises for sounding right). Our work is

  argued from the aviation checklist standard ("rarity and stress"). His objection — *"we're not in
  aviation"* — is right, and the disanalogy is deeper than he put it: **a pilot has memory, a session has
  none**, so the experience axis the whole standard rests on does not apply here at all. The conclusion

  files for one moment. One file per situation (21) was rejected — 21 loads instead of one.

  **"מנהל-העבודה ומבקר-האיכות"**, opening line *"אתה מוביל את העבודה ואינך כותב קוד — ואתה שער-האיכות
  היחיד: כל דבר שנכנס, אתה בדקת בעצמך."*

  swung to "this should shrink to a paragraph" — under-correcting past the truth to demonstrate
  responsiveness. Ishay's *"ולמה לא מצב בעצם?"* restored it. **The situation stayed; the scaffolding
  (blast-radius tables, reference-graph counts) came out — that is craft, not rules.**
  **What it carries:** ongoing upkeep, not an audit · scope asked **after** measuring · **the cluster
  decides batch size, never a constant** (*"אולי 1 אולי 10 — לא יודע, בגלל זה יצרתי מנהל"*) · and
  three gates — **deletion means moving to `docs/delete/`, Ishay deletes** · harvest before shrinking ·
  **`~/.claude/**` never in a REG-IN batch, it changes 710 too.**
- **The idle window (Ishay's idea, sharpened):** after dispatching, the manager does not wait for a
  trigger — **that is the only reading slot the role ever gets**, and it is why the §7 backlog, the
  doc debt and the 896-line M4 research doc keep being deferred. Three guards make it safe:
  **a builder message always pre-empts the reading** (the prompt promises a responsive manager) ·
  **analyse now, present later, and re-measure first** (un-remeasured options are the stale board) ·
  and **nothing can start ⇒ one line, not a menu.** Plus the quiet trap: an idle manager who reads
  will find things, and finding tempts him to justify the time — **"לא בכוח" applies; "אין" is a
  complete answer.**
- ✅ **MIGRATION COMPLETE — the manager skill is now organised by situation, not by topic.**

### 01/08/2026 — **"עדכן ושלח" without changes no longer saves — and the brief's own wording was wrong** (feature, Ishay's ruling)
- **The bug:** no change detection, so an empty update ran a full `update`; `moddatetime` bumped
  `updated_at`; **expiry derives from `updated_at`** ⇒ a quote with two days left silently went
  back to 30.
- **🔴 Contradiction raised before code:** the brief wanted a confirm saying *"sending resets the
  validity"* — but with the save skipped nothing resets it (the send path never writes to
  `quotes`; verified by grep). The requested sentence would have been **false information the
  user decides on.** Manager ruled (א) — validity preserved — citing the code's own rationale
  (*the clock renews because of the price, not the send*) and a market check.
- **The asymmetry is the design:** erring "changed" = a redundant save; erring "unchanged" =
  **swallowing the user's work**. Every doubt resolves to "changed" (unparseable value, unexpected
  shape, missing snapshot, any throw), locked by 6 tests that each assert `true` on a case that
  could plausibly have read as "unchanged".
- **Compares form to the loaded snapshot, not to the DB row** — same `quoteToFormState` on both
  sides, so no conversion asymmetry. Catalog-derived fields excluded, or a **price-list change
  between two page loads** would count as a user edit.
- **Verified live both ways and checked in the DB:** no-change ⇒ exact sentence, **0 writes**,
  dialog opens; with a change ⇒ no confirm, 1 write. `quotes#7.updated_at` read back unchanged.
  E2E guard has a positive control and was **watched failing** with the branch disabled.
- 410 unit · build green · `quotes.spec.js` 18/18 · zero DB writes.
- Full narrative: `docs/micro_guides/module-3.md` §9 (01/08).

### 01/08/2026 — **"שמור ושלח": the connection the spec always had and the build never wired** (feature, manager-approved)
- **Ishay surfaced it and was right about the shape:** the dialog was *already mounted* on the
  builder page, just fed the live form (no status ⇒ `isQuoteSendable` false ⇒ no send button).
  Save navigated straight to the list. A connection problem, not a missing feature.
- **Three silent seams:** `createQuote`'s returned id was discarded · `getQuote` doesn't join
  `customers` (no recipient ⇒ button born disabled) · `emailTemplate`/`canEdit` never passed.
  None throws. Injected the customer from the page rather than widening `getQuote`, whose contract
  is documented **and locked by a unit test**.
- **Two `try` blocks now, and it is a requirement:** the old single `catch` says "שמירת ההצעה
  נכשלה", so a document failure after a successful save would have claimed the save failed — and a
  user who believes it saves again and gets a duplicate.
- **Re-fetch needed in EDIT too:** `savedQuote` is load-time only, and the document's validity
  window derives from `updated_at` — stale would print the previous version's expiry to a customer.
- **E2E, with a mine the brief missed:** a real save test would add a quote row **every gate run**
  to the live DB. Intercepted `create_quote` + the re-fetch ⇒ zero writes, plus an RPC counter so it
  can't pass on nothing. Watched failing on the old behaviour. Caught a bug in my own assertion
  (`toHaveAttribute('title','')` vs an *absent* attribute).
- **Also landed (Ishay's rulings):** label → `שמור ושלח`/`עדכן ושלח`; and **"טרם נשלחה" brought to
  `QuotesPage`** — from a finding I raised unprompted: `getSentQuoteIds` had a single call site, so
  the indicator existed on the customer card but not where quotes are managed. One batched query,
  identical vocabulary, `in_progress` only, three-state (failed log = "unknown", never "not sent").
  All four branches verified live, incl. the positive one the real data cannot show (proven by
  interception, not assumed).
- **Regression measured, not assumed:** 384 unit · build green · full E2E has **8 pre-existing
  failures from quote #6's accidental approval** — proven by stashing this work and re-running.
  **Wider than the 3 previously measured.** A 9th varies per run, green in isolation (load-flake).
- **Demo delta:** `quotes#22` — the live create-path check cannot be done without one real save.

### 01/08/2026 — **Fix-round item 3: PDF BiDi — three reported defects, one root cause, and a planned fix that would have been a no-op** (fix, work-manager-approved plan)
- **The plan's own hypothesis was half-wrong, and reading the library caught it before any code.**
  Plan said "add `direction:'rtl'` to `styles.page`". `@react-pdf/layout`'s
  `BASE_INHERITABLE_PROPERTIES` contains `textAlign` but **not** `direction`, and `getFragments`
  reads it off each `<Text>`'s own style with a hard `'ltr'` default ⇒ the Page-level fix would
  have changed **nothing, silently**, and the visual check afterwards could easily have been
  read as "partly worked". Correct fix: a `RTL` const spread into every Hebrew text style.
- **All three defects were one mechanism.** `textAlign:'right'` is alignment, not direction;
  with no RTL base, neutrals (period, parens, digits) migrate to the wrong edge. Measured
  before→after: terms periods moved line-start→line-end, "הנדון" reordered, "30 יום" clean.
- **The fix introduced a real bug, caught by measurement.** `<Ltr>` built `[{direction:'ltr'},
  style]` — so `styles.pairVal`'s new `rtl` **won**, flipping ח"פ/טלפון/תאריך/שעות at once (the
  double-flip the work-manager predicted). `<Ltr>` now writes its direction last; `CELL_GAP`
  deliberately excluded from `RTL` for the same reason.
- **2 new unit guards, both watched failing on mutations** (7 texts flagged / the 4 latin values
  flagged by name). They verify the **mechanism**, not glyph order — stated in the test file, because
  an assertion pretending to measure visual order from the text layer would pass on a broken document.
- **Money re-verified per the manager's condition:** real quote #21 re-rendered read-only —
  6,300→-315→-630→5,355→964→**6,319 ₪** verbatim. **378 unit · 73/73 E2E** · lint/format clean.
  A `quote-email` flake was **not** waved away (that test asserts on real PDF bytes) — re-run twice, both green.
- **DoD "PDF RTL" box deliberately left UNCHECKED** — closes only on Ishay's own eye, per the standing condition.
- Full narrative: `docs/micro_guides/module-3.md` §9 (01/08, fix-round item 3).

### 01/08/2026 — **Quote builder: fix-round item 1, silent-panel bug on discount>100%** (fix, work-manager-scoped)
- **What:** audit finding, scoped down by Ishay before the fix ("no-draft is intentional, the
  save-block on illegal discount numbers is correct and stays — only the missing explanation was
  the bug"). `computeQuoteTotals` (`pricing.js`) throws when the two discounts sum past 100% (a
  typo like 100 instead of 10). `QuoteBuilderPage.jsx`'s catch turned that into `totals=null`, and
  `{totals && <QuoteSummaryPanel/>}` made the **whole panel — including the Save button — vanish
  with zero explanation**, and the existing `errors.manualDiscount` message could never reach the
  screen either (it's gated on `submitAttempted`, only set by the Save button that had just
  disappeared).
- **Fix:** render a red explanatory box (`data-testid="quote-totals-blocked"`) in the panel's slot
  instead of nothing; Save stays unreachable in that state (unchanged, was already correct).
- **Proof:** new `e2e/quotes.spec.js` test — watched it **fail on the pre-fix code** (`git stash`
  of the one-line-scoped diff, re-run, confirmed red), then pass after restoring the fix. Full
  `quotes.spec.js` suite (12 tests) green. Live-verified logged in as CEO in the browser: message
  renders in RTL, panel/Save return once the discount is corrected. `eslint` clean.
- Full narrative: `docs/micro_guides/module-3.md` §9 (01/08, fix-round item 1).

- **Measured, not felt.** `work-manager` is 237 lines — ~2.9× the next-largest repo skill, and it
  grew 150 → 237 in six hours with nothing ever removed. Two prunes were drafted (170 and 136
  lines) and three role-based evals run against both — sequencing/absorption · decision-guarding ·
  knowing-when-*not*-to-act. **Both prunes scored identically on all three.**
- **Ishay's ruling: do not prune.** The cost was never measured before assuming it: 237 lines is
  ~3–4K tokens loaded once per management conversation. No measurable harm, the skill demonstrably
  works, and module 3's deadline is 7 days out. Both drafts kept in the session scratchpad so a
  later prune starts from measured ground.
- 🔑 **The epistemics Ishay supplied, now written into the skill:** *"a prune that leaves the evals
  green proves only that the evals do not look there — absence of evidence is not evidence of
  absence."* Evals can **falsify** a prune, never authorise one; every deletion needs its own
  reason. Two cuts flagged as especially costly: removing the **why** and keeping the rule, and
  removing a **rare** rule (nothing catches its absence until the day it mattered).
- **What went in instead of a prune** — two questions that stop the growth rather than reverse it:
  *"is this true almost always, or am I patching a single incident?"* before adding, and
  *"which paragraph here has never once changed a decision?"* occasionally, in reverse.
- **Byproduct worth more than the experiment:** the eval agents, working on unrelated questions,
  surfaced four real repo defects — the lost warning-3 (above), the `19:2x` placeholder, the
  future-dated STATUS header, and **E2E never running in CI while 81 selectors match literal
  Hebrew strings**. The last one is unresolved and needs Ishay.

### 31/07/2026 18:35 — **Step 4.1 re-tagged 🤖→👤, and the rule that made the fix belong in the guide** (docs only)
- **The defect:** step 4.1 was tagged 🔻🤖 ("Claude verifies alone and continues") in both the
  step table and the step body — while a successful approval is **irreversible**. Measured:
  trigger `quotes_lock_non_in_progress` blocks `update` **and** `delete` once status leaves
  `in_progress`, and `projects.quote_id … on delete restrict` locks it from the other side.
  No un-approve path exists, and there is one live Supabase project. A session following the
  guide faithfully would have approved a quote "to check" and changed the demo data forever.
- **Fixed in the guide, not in a prompt** — and that distinction is the actual lesson. I had
  written a long hand-off prompt carrying this warning; Ishay pushed back: *"corrections to
  future steps belong in the guide, not the prompt."* He was right, and it exposed an
  inconsistency in my own work an hour apart — I fixed the 4.3 gap **in the guide** and then
  fixed the 4.1 gap **in a prompt**. Same class of problem, opposite treatments.
- **Ruling recorded next to iron rule 15** (`docs/CLAUDE.md`): a discovery affecting a step that
  hasn't started goes into the guide, same session. The practical test: knowledge still true in
  a month (how the DB behaves, what is irreversible, what was removed) → guide; only a freshness
  stamp (numbers that moved today) → prompt. What it subtracts: the long repeated prompts, and
  the dependency on someone remembering to attach them.
- The step body now splits 4.1 explicitly: **failure paths are safe to build unasked** (every
  rejected approval ends in `raise exception` ⇒ transaction rolls back, zero DB change), while
  the **success path is a 👤 stop** — and the seed's one already-approved quote can prove
  "project born complete" read-only, without creating anything.

### 31/07/2026 18:20 — **Monitor discipline + a status board, from gedood-710's field use** (skill file only)
- **Self-monitoring replaced waiting to be told.** Ishay asked "can't you check yourself?" — he
  was right, and the skill had been built around him relaying "the session finished", i.e. making
  him a courier between two sessions. Now: a background loop exiting on **two conditions
  together** (HEAD moved *and* clean tree, `--untracked-files=no`). Neither alone is sufficient.
- **`git fetch` removed from the loop** — both sessions share one disk, so a commit is local the
  instant it lands. It was the only costly step and the only reason to poll slowly; without it
  the interval is chosen by how fast you want to know (~2 min per fix round).
- 🔴 **Trap that is worse here than at 710: every commit in this repo carries the same git
  identity**, mine and the builders'. Author-based filtering — the obvious fix — does not exist
  as an option. Discipline instead: re-arm `BASE` after any commit of my own, and `git log -1`
  before reporting "it landed". **Measured near-miss:** the monitor was armed 17:47, I committed
  `9e35272` at 17:49 — HEAD moved, so condition one fired. The alert stayed silent **only because
  the builder's tree was dirty**. The two-condition rule absorbed it; that is luck, not a
  guarantee — it would fail if I committed during a quiet moment.
- **Adopted an "איפה עומדים" closing board** (4–6 measured rows: running · just closed · free to
  start now · deadline · needs-Ishay), with the constraint that makes it safe: **every row
  measured the same turn or marked טעון בדיקה.** Written into the skill as the most dangerous
  artifact of the role — it reads authoritative, Ishay acts on it directly, and a stale
  "free to start" row sends him into a collision with a live session.

Registered as 3 rows in `schema_migrations`; the file header carries the full account.
- **Verification worth reusing — the rate limit was proven with ZERO permanent rows.** Ishay had approved test-row injection into the live DB; it turned out to be unnecessary. A single `DO` block sets `request.headers` **transaction-locally** (`set_config(..., true)`), calls the RPC 16×, then `raise`s to roll itself back. Output: *"הקריאה הראשונה שנחסמה: 16 … אחרי איפוס היומן: עברה ✅"* — 15 pass, the 16th is blocked, and **deleting the log rows removes the block**, which is what proves causation rather than coincidence. **This pattern replaces live-row injection for anything that reads `request.headers`.**
- **Embed shape was measured, not assumed** (the plan-review flagged it): PostgREST returns an **object or null** for the one-to-one `product_costs` embed. An array would have made every cost `null` **with no error** — profit = full revenue. `flattenProductCost` (`src/lib/catalog.js`, shared by both API layers per rule 14) maps a missing cost to **`null`, never `0`** — same "unknown ≠ zero" distinction as the VAT guard, and both new unit tests were watched failing on a `?? 0` version.
- **Follow-up worth knowing:** a full E2E run makes exactly one failed login, so 15/IP/hour permits 15 full runs per hour — the suite cannot rate-limit itself.

### 31/07/2026 10:47 — Audit round **B** CLOSED: three `catch` blocks that silently disabled safety nets
- **What changed & why:** in all three, a *load failure* was indistinguishable from *"loaded, nothing here"* — and each one thereby switched off a guard built after a real incident. (1) `CustomersPage`'s revenue `catch` wrote `{}`, so `handleToggleStatus` mapped every customer to `openCount:0`, `archiveWarningMessage` returned null, and the §7.34 archive warning **vanished entirely** — now an explicit `null` (not "leave as-is": the effect re-runs on every `reloadTick`) + `revenueLoadFailed` + a non-blocking amber banner with retry. (2) `QuoteDocumentDialog`'s `.catch(() => {})` made the window declare "not sent yet" **on the strength of a failure** — `previousSend` is now tri-state (`undefined`/`null`/row) and unknown opens a confirm; the three strings live in `src/lib/email.js` as an engine contract for M4/M8/M11. (3) `AuthContext` never captured the permissions error ⇒ empty map ⇒ `ProtectedRoute` said "אין לך הרשאה" — a screen identical to a real denial, on a code path that re-runs on **every token refresh**.
- **Sibling path handled, not deferred:** `send-email` logged `email_log` insert failures to `console.error` and still returned `ok:true` — the mail went out and the double-send guard died unseen. Response now carries `log_failed` (additive; old clients unaffected), surfaced in the dialog. Deployed as **version 3**, verified by diffing the source the server returns.
- **Bonus fix in the same function, same failure family and worse:** any error on the `users` query triggered a full `signOut` + "your account is not authorized" — a one-second network blip **ejected a working user and blamed them**. Only `PGRST116` (no row) now signs out.
- **Every guard proven by returning the failure, per warning 3** — not by watching it pass. With `{}` restored, the archive confirm never appears (the customer would be silently archived); with `previousSend` back at `null`, **no question is asked at all and the click goes straight to sending** (that negative run was executed with `functions/v1` blocked so no real mail could leave); with the permissions branch disabled, the old denial screen returns. All three restored and re-verified green.
- **Regression:** `npm run gate` **exit 0** · **353 unit** (was 345) · **E2E 24/24, zero skips** (was 21) · new permanent spec `e2e/load-failure-guards.spec.js` — route-interception only, plus one test customer created and deleted, because all four live customers hold an open quote so there was no "clean customer" case for the regression half.
- ⚠️ **A screenshot nearly produced a false finding.** The permissions-failure capture showed an almost-empty sidebar, contradicting my own plan note that the sidebar does *not* empty (`Sidebar.jsx` filters only `'blocked'`). Measured instead of assumed: the `modules` request returns **200 with all 7 rows** and the sidebar renders all 8 links — the screenshot had caught it mid-load. The plan's correction stands; the artifact was timing.
- **A 4th site of the same family, fixed after Ishay pushed back on deferring it — and he was right.** I had classified `getSentQuoteIds` in `CustomerDetailsPage` as display-only. It isn't: an empty Set on failure renders **"טרם נשלחה ללקוח" on a quote that was in fact sent**, and that amber line is precisely the cue that makes a person open the dialog and send. `sentIds` is now `null`-when-unknown, both badges disappear, and a notice takes their place. Proven the same way: with `new Set()` restored the test fails. **The lesson: "display-only" is not a property of the data, it's a claim about what the user does next** — a label that drives an irreversible action is a guard.
- **A 5th site, found only because I tried to test the 4th.** Writing the E2E for the transient-`users` branch, it failed against my own new code: `LoginPage` **queries `users` itself** right after Auth, so it hits the failure first and `AuthContext`'s branch is never reached on the login path. Its `if (dbError || !userData)` told a legitimate user *"משתמש זה אינו מורשה במערכת"* and signed them out — the same accusation, one layer earlier and more visible. Both now split on `PGRST116`, verified empirically against the live DB (`.single()` on zero rows returns exactly that code). **Evergreen: a fix you cannot reach from the UI is not a fix — the test that tries to reach it is what proves the path.**
- **Two assumptions turned into facts rather than left as reasoning:** `PGRST116` is what `.single()` returns for zero rows (queried live), and `functions.invoke` parses `application/json` into `data` (read in `@supabase/functions-js`), which is what makes `fnData.log_failed` readable at all.

Also verified their second correction: `7ac1e47` (the hash I recorded) exists as an object but is **on no branch** — they amended the message after I had already cited it. Real hash: `2f8824c`, 18 files. **The lesson is mine and it is the project's own rule 4:** a claim about external state gets checked in the same turn, not inferred from a symptom.
- **Precondition block written at the head of prompt B** (outside the copy-paste fence, so it is read before pasting rather than swallowed): one live writing session only — with *this* incident named as the evidence, since B touches two of the same files round A did · working tree clean first (`docs/PROJECT_MASTER.md` is still dangling from the §6 email-engine write and must be committed before or with B) · the baseline it departs from (`2f8824c`, gate 0, 345 tests, E2E 21/21) · **and the known-flaky `permissions.spec.js` test**, flagged explicitly so that if it fails during B nobody hunts a regression that isn't there.
- **And he corrected my proposed fix.** I had written "a view without `cost`"; he asked whether splitting in two would help. Checked: **Supabase explicitly recommends *against* column-level privileges**, and their core discussions land on *"splitting sensitive columns into separate tables with RLS policies"* as the clearest approach — a `security_invoker` view **cannot** restrict columns, so my direction would not have worked. Fix-plan §C(3) rewritten to the split (⚠️ the **table**, not the screen — the leak is at REST level, and the prices screen is already CEO-only), with the three read sites flagged, plus the one the split must not miss: `approve_quote_and_create_project` reads `products.cost` directly.
